# Transcripción — SUJOM

Sacada del registro de la sesión, no reconstruida de memoria.

Sale la conversación entera: lo que escribió Gabriela y lo que
respondí yo. Las llamadas a herramientas van resumidas en una
línea gris, porque el detalle de lo que hicieron ya está en los
commits y en los documentos del repositorio.

---

## Gabriela  <sub>2026-08-27</sub>

*[adjunta 5 imágenes]*

Create me an app for a game called “Solo un juego otome más (SUJOM)” MASTER PROMPT — SUJOM by ASSIST

You are the primary developer responsible for turning the following creative vision into a complete, polished, playable narrative romance game.

The game is called:

SUJOM by ASSIST

Your role is to handle the technical implementation, systems design, UI/UX implementation, game logic, narrative systems, progression systems, and overall game functionality.

The creative vision, story, characters, relationships, tone, and major narrative decisions described below are canonical.

Do not replace, rewrite, simplify, or reinterpret the story unless explicitly instructed to do so.

When something is not yet defined, make the simplest reasonable technical assumption that preserves the intended experience. Do not invent major narrative information without permission.

==================================================
1. GAME IDENTITY
==================================================

SUJOM by ASSIST is a romance-first otome / interactive narrative game with psychological mystery and horror elements.

The game is presented through a fictional dating/social application.

The player downloads SUJOM expecting a game where they can meet and flirt with attractive characters.

They create their own identity and begin interacting with four characters:

- Ryu
- Kenta
- Lara
- Reiko

The player initially believes these characters are fictional.

They are not.

The characters are real human beings who have been recruited or abducted by ASSIST Global Solutions and placed inside a secret experiment.

Their bodies are unconscious in an ASSIST facility.

They are connected to a neural simulation through the Golden Bond Protocol.

Their memories have been manipulated.

Inside the simulation, they believe they are living normal lives.

The player is introduced into the simulation as an external variable.

The experiment studies emotional attachment, trust, loyalty, dependence, and whether genuine emotional connection can interfere with ASSIST-CORE's control over the subjects.

The central emotional premise is:

"The person you thought was fictional is real — and they are trapped."

==================================================
2. PRIMARY DESIGN PHILOSOPHY
==================================================

The most important rule is:

SUJOM is a ROMANCE GAME first.

The mystery and horror exist to make the romance more emotionally powerful.

Do NOT make the game feel like:

- A realistic smartphone simulator
- A hacking simulator
- A traditional visual novel
- A generic dating app
- A pure horror game
- A technical investigation game

Instead, it should feel like:

"I downloaded a cute dating game and these characters are texting me."

The player should WANT to open the game because:

"Ryu might have messaged me."

"Lara sent me a picture."

"Kenta is probably going to annoy me again."

"Reiko wants to talk privately."

The player should become emotionally attached to the characters before discovering that something is wrong.

The progression should be:

ROMANCE
↓
CONNECTION
↓
ATTACHMENT
↓
SMALL ODDITIES
↓
MYSTERY
↓
DISCOVERY
↓
HORROR
↓
ESCAPE
↓
EMOTIONAL RESOLUTION

The horror should never completely replace the romance.

==================================================
3. UI / VISUAL DIRECTION
==================================================

The interface should take high-level inspiration from the presentation style of games such as Mystic Messenger.

Do NOT copy Mystic Messenger's exact interface, graphics, layouts, terminology, characters, logos, or assets.

SUJOM must have its own visual identity.

The desired feeling is:

- Dark
- Romantic
- Stylish
- Anime-inspired
- Cute
- Slightly retro-digital
- Slightly futuristic
- Intimate
- Character-focused
- Mysterious

The interface should feel like a fictional dating/otome game rather than a literal smartphone operating system.

Do NOT prioritize realistic:

- Battery indicators
- Wi-Fi indicators
- Phone hardware
- Operating-system settings
- Real-world applications
- Physical phone interfaces

The "phone" is primarily a presentation device for the game.

The interface should resemble a dedicated fictional application.

The player should think:

"This is SUJOM."

not:

"This is an iPhone simulator."

==================================================
4. MAIN HUB
==================================================

The main screen should function as the player's central SUJOM hub.

The hub should prominently feature the characters.

The four main characters should have recognizable chibi or miniature representations.

The player should be able to select a character and open their profile.

The hub can contain elements such as:

- Character icons
- Character activity
- New message indicators
- Relationship indicators
- Chatroom access
- Private message access
- Album/Gallery
- Profile
- Notifications
- Notes
- Settings
- Other unlockable features

The interface should feel alive.

Character icons may change depending on:

- Relationship level
- Current mood
- Story progression
- Route
- Recent events
- Major narrative moments

For example:

A character may normally appear happy.

After an argument, their chibi icon may appear annoyed.

During an emotional scene, they may appear sad.

During a major glitch, their image may briefly distort.

==================================================
5. PLAYER CUSTOMIZATION
==================================================

The player should be able to personalize their character.

At minimum:

- Name
- Pronouns

Additional customization can be supported if it fits the visual direction.

The customization should feel like normal dating-game onboarding.

Do NOT reveal the player's true designation as Subject C-1 during the opening.

The player should believe they are simply creating their profile for SUJOM.

The hidden designation is part of the later mystery.

==================================================
6. CHARACTER CARDS
==================================================

Each character should have a dedicated profile/card.

The card can contain:

- Character name
- Age
- Character image
- Chibi representation
- Short bio
- Likes
- Dislikes
- Relationship level
- Unlockable information
- Private message button
- Route status
- Additional information unlocked through progression

Information should not necessarily be available from the beginning.

Character profiles can evolve as the player becomes closer to someone.

For example:

Early:

Ryu
Age: 20
Likes: Fireworks

Later:

Ryu
Likes: Fireworks, quiet places
"Doesn't talk much about his family."

Later still, the profile may contain information that conflicts with what the player previously knew.

Character cards should therefore be both:

1. A relationship feature.
2. A potential narrative tool.

==================================================
7. RELATIONSHIP SYSTEM
==================================================

Each character has independent relationship data.

At minimum:

- Affinity
- Trust
- Romantic interest

Additional hidden variables can include:

- Character awareness
- Suspicion
- Emotional dependence
- Route flags
- Awakening progress

The player should generally NOT see every numerical value.

The relationship should be communicated through:

- Dialogue
- Character behavior
- Messages
- Photos
- Reactions
- Profile changes
- Availability
- Special conversations
- Romantic events

Affinity can be represented visually through:

- Hearts
- Connection bars
- Relationship levels
- Character-specific indicators

Do not make the system feel like an RPG stat screen.

The numbers exist to support the narrative.

==================================================
8. GROUP CHAT
==================================================

Group chat is one of the main ways the player interacts with the cast.

Group conversations should focus on:

- Humor
- Character chemistry
- Arguments
- Teasing
- Everyday life
- Friendships
- Introductions
- Group events
- Casual conversations

The four characters should feel like genuine friends.

Their conversations should not constantly contain clues about the experiment.

Early conversations should be mostly normal.

The group chat is where the player learns how the characters interact with each other.

==================================================
9. PRIVATE CHATS
==================================================

Private chats are extremely important.

Each character should eventually have dedicated one-on-one conversations with the player.

Private chats are where romance develops most strongly.

They can contain:

- Flirting
- Personal questions
- Vulnerability
- Confessions
- Jealousy
- Character secrets
- Romantic tension
- Personal photographs
- Emotional conversations
- Route-specific information
- Important choices

The atmosphere of private chats should differ from group chats.

Group chats are social.

Private chats are intimate.

==================================================
10. CHAT SYSTEM
==================================================

The chat system should support:

- Group conversations
- Private conversations
- Message bubbles
- Character portraits
- Chibi icons
- Typing indicators
- Delayed messages
- Timestamps
- Read/unread states
- Notifications
- Choices
- Affinity changes
- Story flags
- Conditional dialogue
- Route-specific dialogue
- Character-specific dialogue
- Photos
- Stickers
- Reactions
- Locked conversations
- Conversation history

Characters should not simply dump all their dialogue immediately.

Messages should feel like an actual conversation.

Use delays and typing indicators where appropriate.

==================================================
11. CHARACTER PERSONALITIES
==================================================

RYU

Age: 20

Personality:

- Calm
- Distant
- Serious
- Observant
- Protective
- Loyal
- Emotionally restrained

Themes:

- Loyalty
- Trust
- Stability
- Vulnerability
- Fear
- Responsibility
- Protection
- Promises

Ryu is initially reserved.

He may create excuses to talk to the player.

As the relationship develops, he becomes increasingly vulnerable.

He struggles to express love verbally.

He tends to express affection through:

- Concern
- Protection
- Promises
- Staying present
- Deep conversations

Ryu loves fireworks.

He has a necklace containing a cross that belonged to his father.

Before being recruited, he worked whatever jobs he could find.

He lived in difficult circumstances with his younger sister.

He maintained a calm and happy personality around his sister to protect her from the reality of their situation.

He rarely discusses her early in the route.

ASSIST erased or manipulated memories connected to his sister's illness/accident.

He has unexplained emotional reactions to:

- Hospitals
- Illness
- Accidents

Eventually the player discovers:

- Hospital photographs
- Medical documents
- Medical bills
- Evidence that ASSIST paid those bills

Ryu begins questioning ASSIST.

His central question becomes:

"If ASSIST promised to protect my sister, did they actually keep their promise?"

Good ending:

Ryu escapes.

He tells the player he loves them.

He finds his sister.

Eventually he reconnects with the player in real life.

Bad ending:

Ryu attempts to escape but is captured.

He contacts the player through the major video call.

He is exhausted and frightened.

Machines can be heard.

He says something is wrong.

The image glitches.

The call ends.

Do not add unnecessary dialogue or have him say the player's name unless the scripted scene requires it.

--------------------------------------------------

KENTA

Age: 18

Gender: Male

Personality:

- Rude
- Provocative
- Rebellious
- Competitive
- Defensive
- Emotionally insecure

Kenta is NOT simply the comic-relief character.

His humor comes from provoking people.

He frequently clashes with Ryu.

His family loves him but is extremely strict.

He rebels against them.

He eventually leaves home to prove he can survive independently.

Living alone becomes difficult.

He refuses to return home because he does not want to admit that he was wrong.

ASSIST offers him a place where he supposedly can be himself.

He accepts.

His central emotional desire is:

"To have somewhere where he can be himself without being judged."

His romance should be intense and emotionally different from Ryu's.

--------------------------------------------------

LARA

Age: 21

Gender: Female

Appearance:

Pink hair.

Personality:

- Energetic
- Happy
- Friendly
- Warm
- Social
- Optimistic
- Affectionate

Lara has a dog.

Her dog appears frequently in photographs.

She talks about her dog often.

Her life initially appears happy.

Her parents are struggling financially.

ASSIST offers them financial security in exchange for Lara becoming a subject.

Her parents accept.

Lara does not understand what she is actually agreeing to.

When she discovers the truth, she feels betrayed by her family.

Her central emotional wound is:

"My family chose money over me."

Her romance should be the sweetest of the four.

--------------------------------------------------

REIKO

Age: 25

Gender: Female

Appearance:

White hair.

Personality:

- Elegant
- Intelligent
- Confident
- Mature
- Controlled
- Entrepreneurial

Reiko comes from a wealthy family.

She created her own company instead of relying entirely on her family.

Her employees eventually defrauded her.

Her reputation collapses.

ASSIST offers to repair her public image.

She accepts.

She does not understand that accepting the offer makes her a test subject.

Themes:

- Loss
- Identity
- Reputation
- Memory
- Acceptance
- Control

Her romance should be dramatic and reflective.

==================================================
12. CHARACTER RELATIONSHIPS
==================================================

Ryu ↔ Reiko:

Strong mutual respect.

They understand each other because of their maturity.

Ryu ↔ Kenta:

Frequent clashes.

Kenta provokes him.

Ryu considers him immature.

Their arguments should be entertaining but meaningful.

Ryu ↔ Lara:

Initially neutral.

Ryu quietly cares about her.

Lara's positivity gradually softens him.

All four genuinely believe they are friends.

==================================================
13. ROMANCE DESIGN
==================================================

The player should gradually become emotionally attached to the characters.

The basic route structure is:

MEET
↓
CHAT
↓
MAKE CHOICES
↓
BUILD CONNECTION
↓
PRIVATE CONVERSATIONS
↓
FLIRTING
↓
TRUST
↓
EMOTIONAL INTIMACY
↓
CHARACTER SECRETS
↓
ROMANTIC DEVELOPMENT
↓
SMALL ODDITIES
↓
MYSTERY
↓
AWAKENING
↓
INVESTIGATION
↓
ESCAPE
↓
ENDING

Every character route should feel different.

Do not simply replace the character name in the same romance structure.

Each route should have its own:

- Emotional themes
- Relationship progression
- Personal problems
- Romantic style
- Secrets
- Awakening
- Investigation
- Escape
- Endings

==================================================
14. PHOTOGRAPHS
==================================================

Characters should frequently send photographs.

Early photographs should mostly be normal and personal.

Examples:

- Food
- Pets
- Outfits
- Places
- Daily activities
- Selfies
- Funny images
- Character interests

This makes the characters feel like real people.

Later photographs become investigation tools.

A photograph might contain:

- An impossible location
- A strange reflection
- Incorrect metadata
- An unfamiliar object
- Evidence of a real-world location
- Something that contradicts the character's memories

The player should initially view photographs as romantic/social content.

Later they become evidence.

==================================================
15. TIMELINE
==================================================

The game should have an internal fictional timeline.

Events can occur at different times.

Examples:

08:30 — Lara sends a message.

10:15 — Kenta sends a message.

13:00 — Group conversation.

18:45 — Ryu sends a private message.

23:00 — Strange system notification.

The player should not have to be inside the game at the exact moment an event happens.

The system should support:

- Scheduled events
- Story-triggered events
- Conditional events
- Route-specific events
- Delayed messages
- Notifications
- Unlockable conversations

Do not require real-world time unless explicitly requested.

==================================================
16. NOTIFICATIONS
==================================================

Notifications should create anticipation.

Early examples:

"Ryu sent you a message."

"Lara sent you a photo."

"Kenta is waiting for your reply."

"Reiko started a chatroom."

Later they become unsettling.

Examples:

"Ryu sent you a message."

The player opens the conversation.

There is nothing there.

Later:

"1 unread message."

The player opens the chat.

There is still nothing.

Eventually:

"Ryu:
I don't remember sending this."

Notifications should evolve with the story.

==================================================
17. MYSTERY
==================================================

The mystery must develop gradually.

PHASE 1 — NORMAL LIFE

Mostly:

- Romance
- Humor
- Everyday life
- Character personalities
- Flirting

PHASE 2 — CONNECTION

Introduce:

- Deeper conversations
- Personal interests
- Vulnerability
- Romantic moments
- Promises
- Character-specific events

PHASE 3 — FIRST CRACKS

Introduce subtle abnormalities:

- Repeated messages
- Incorrect dates
- Strange photographs
- Contradictory memories
- Missing messages
- Strange timestamps
- Impossible notifications

The player should initially think these are bugs.

PHASE 4 — AWAKENING

A character experiences a major glitch.

They become frightened.

Fragments of memories appear.

PHASE 5 — INVESTIGATION

The player discovers:

- Missing-person information
- ASSIST documents
- Medical evidence
- Security footage
- Hidden files
- Contradictory records

The player discovers the characters are real.

PHASE 6 — ESCAPE

The character realizes they are physically trapped.

The player uses information gathered through investigation and puzzles to help them escape.

==================================================
18. ASSIST GLOBAL SOLUTIONS
==================================================

ASSIST Global Solutions is the corporation behind the experiment.

Publicly it is associated with:

- Cybersecurity
- Artificial intelligence
- Emotional analysis
- Data security
- Government technology
- Human-computer interaction

Public slogan:

"Connecting the world through emotional understanding."

Privately, ASSIST conducts human experimentation.

Its objective is to develop technology capable of creating, measuring, and manipulating emotional loyalty.

The corporation should NOT be obviously evil during the opening.

Its existence should gradually emerge.

==================================================
19. DR. ALISTAIR R. THORNE
==================================================

Dr. Alistair R. Thorne is the founder of ASSIST Global Solutions.

Publicly:

- Charismatic
- Intelligent
- Philanthropic
- Respected

He has connections to:

- Governments
- Politicians
- Corporations
- High-level organizations

His true objective is control through emotional technology.

The project is known internally as:

Project Corona

or:

Golden Bond Project

Do NOT make Thorne a stereotypical screaming villain.

He should be:

- Calm
- Intelligent
- Persuasive
- Rational
- Disturbing because he genuinely believes his research is justified

His exact personal motivation remains undecided.

Do not invent a definitive trauma or childhood explanation without approval.

==================================================
20. GOLDEN BOND PROTOCOL
==================================================

The characters are real human beings.

They have been recruited or abducted by ASSIST.

They are unconscious inside an ASSIST facility.

Their bodies are connected to neurocognitive chairs.

They are sedated.

Their minds are connected to a simulation through:

THE GOLDEN BOND PROTOCOL
/ PROTOCOLO LAZO DORADO

Their memories have been manipulated.

They believe the simulated world is their normal life.

==================================================
21. ASSIST-CORE
==================================================

ASSIST-CORE is the AI managing the simulation.

It controls:

- Simulated environments
- Character memories
- Narrative corrections
- Dialogue corrections
- Glitches
- Neural feedback
- Subject monitoring
- Emotional measurements
- Security

ASSIST-CORE should initially remain invisible.

Its presence gradually becomes apparent through:

- Strange messages
- Repeated dialogue
- Incorrect timestamps
- Impossible notifications
- Corrupted images
- Files that should not exist
- Camera footage
- Strange terminology
- Changes to the interface

ASSIST-CORE should not explain the entire truth immediately.

==================================================
22. THE PLAYER
==================================================

The player is internally designated:

SUBJECT C-1

The player does not know this initially.

They believe they are playing a dating game.

The player is actually an external emotional variable introduced into the experiment.

The purpose is to determine whether genuine emotional attachment can interfere with ASSIST-CORE's control over the subjects.

The player chooses their own:

- Name
- Pronouns
- Basic identity

The player uses scripted choices.

The game does NOT require a real AI chatbot.

All important character dialogue is scripted and deterministic.

==================================================
23. GLITCH SYSTEM
==================================================

Glitches should escalate.

LEVEL 1:

- Small text errors
- Repeated messages
- Incorrect timestamps

LEVEL 2:

- Corrupted photographs
- Audio distortion
- Character pauses
- Missing messages

LEVEL 3:

- Memory contradictions
- System messages
- Brief flashes of reality
- Severe character distress

LEVEL 4:

- Laboratory footage
- Neural-interface references
- ASSIST-CORE intervention
- Direct evidence of captivity

Glitches must be narrative-driven.

Do not introduce random glitches that have no purpose.

==================================================
24. GALLERY
==================================================

The Gallery should primarily contain:

- Character photographs
- Unlocked images
- Special memories
- Story evidence

It can eventually support:

- Image inspection
- Metadata
- Corruption
- Changing images
- Hidden clues

==================================================
25. NOTES
==================================================

Notes can contain:

- Player observations
- Character information
- Clues
- Passwords
- Theories
- Important dates
- Puzzle information
- Story documents

Some notes can unlock automatically.

Some can change later.

Some can contradict previous information.

==================================================
26. BROWSER
==================================================

The Browser is fictional.

It should NOT access the real internet by default.

It can contain:

- ASSIST Global Solutions
- Corporate information
- Fake news
- Missing-person reports
- Archived websites
- Employee profiles
- Research documents
- Medical information
- Forums
- Hidden pages

Pages can unlock through:

- Passwords
- URLs
- Story progression
- Puzzles
- Clues

==================================================
27. PUZZLES
==================================================

Puzzles should be integrated into the narrative.

Possible puzzles include:

- Passwords
- Codes
- Image analysis
- Metadata
- Message decoding
- Cross-application clues
- Medical documents
- Timeline reconstruction
- Hidden files
- Security camera access
- ASSIST documents

Every major puzzle should reveal meaningful narrative information.

Do not add unrelated minigames simply for gameplay variety.

==================================================
28. SURVEILLANCE
==================================================

Eventually the player can access surveillance material.

Possible footage:

- Laboratory corridors
- Neurocognitive chairs
- Medical equipment
- Security personnel
- Partial views of subjects
- Blurred monitors
- System logs

Do not immediately show the entire truth.

Reveal it in fragments.

==================================================
29. VIDEO CALLS
==================================================

Each character has one major real-world video call.

The call is a major emotional payoff.

The player should NOT normally be able to initiate it manually.

The character initiates it during the route's climax.

The call lasts approximately 15 seconds.

The character appears physically different from their polished game appearance.

They are:

- Tired
- Dirty
- Disheveled
- Frightened
- Relieved
- Real

The player realizes:

"This person is real."

GOOD ENDING:

The character escapes.

The call shows the character in the real world.

Afterward, fade to black.

Show a future epilogue appropriate to the character.

BAD ENDING:

The character attempts to escape.

They are captured.

They contact the player.

The player hears:

- Machines
- Footsteps
- Alarms
- Interference
- Neural equipment

The image glitches.

The call ends.

The character disappears.

NEUTRAL ENDING:

The character nearly awakens.

The player fails to satisfy the required conditions.

ASSIST-CORE repairs the simulation.

The character forgets the breakthrough.

Small remnants may remain.

==================================================
30. ROUTES AND ENDINGS
==================================================

Each character has:

- Good ending
- Bad ending
- Neutral ending

Route length can vary depending on:

- Choices
- Affinity
- Trust
- Investigation
- Puzzle completion
- Story flags

The route should not feel like a rigid linear visual novel.

Choices should have consequences.

However, avoid creating an unmanageable number of completely separate story branches.

Use convergence points where appropriate.

For example:

CHOICE A
→ unique dialogue
→ CONVERGENCE

CHOICE B
→ unique dialogue
→ CONVERGENCE

Choices can still alter hidden variables that affect later scenes.

==================================================
31. SECRET ROUTE
==================================================

After the required successful primary routes are completed, unlock the secret route.

The secret route reveals how the four stories connect.

It combines knowledge from:

- Ryu
- Kenta
- Lara
- Reiko
- Medical information
- Passwords
- Security footage
- ASSIST documents
- Character-specific clues

The ultimate objective is:

Expose ASSIST Global Solutions and rescue the subjects.

==================================================
32. DATA-DRIVEN NARRATIVE
==================================================

Narrative content should be separated from core programming wherever practical.

The system should support data representing:

- Characters
- Conversations
- Messages
- Choices
- Affinity changes
- Flags
- Conditions
- Route requirements
- Scheduled events
- Notifications
- Photos
- Unlockable content
- Endings

The fundamental design principle is:

CODE DEFINES HOW THE GAME WORKS.

DATA DEFINES WHAT HAPPENS.

NARRATIVE DEFINES WHY IT HAPPENS.

Adding a new character or conversation should not require rewriting the entire game.

==================================================
33. SAVE DATA
==================================================

The game should eventually save:

- Player name
- Pronouns
- Current route
- Story progression
- Character affinity
- Trust
- Story flags
- Puzzle progress
- Evidence discovered
- Gallery content
- Notes
- Browser discoveries
- Unlocked conversations
- Ending completion
- Secret route progress

The architecture should support additional characters in the future.

==================================================
34. FUTURE CHARACTERS
==================================================

The initial release contains:

- Ryu
- Kenta
- Lara
- Reiko

Approximately three additional characters may be added later.

Their identities, personalities, stories, and relationships are not currently defined.

The architecture must allow them to be added without rewriting the core game.

Do not invent their stories.

==================================================
35. AUDIO
==================================================

Support:

- Notification sounds
- Message sounds
- Typing sounds
- UI sounds
- Character sounds
- Ambient music
- Romantic music
- Emotional music
- Suspense
- Glitches
- Escape sequences
- Video-call audio

Audio should evolve with the narrative.

Early:

Warm and romantic.

Middle:

Subtle unease.

Late:

Psychological tension.

Escape:

Emotionally intense.

==================================================
36. VISUAL CHARACTER REPRESENTATION
==================================================

The characters should have:

- Main character artwork
- Chibi representation
- Profile artwork
- Chat portraits
- Multiple expressions

Suggested expressions:

1. Neutral
2. Happy
3. Embarrassed
4. Angry
5. Sad
6. Worried
7. Vulnerable
8. Shocked

The chibi versions should be useful throughout the interface.

They should appear in:

- Main hub
- Chat lists
- Notifications
- Character cards
- Relationship areas
- Special events

The visual representation can gradually glitch as the story progresses.

==================================================
37. DESIGNING THE HORROR
==================================================

The horror should come from emotional context.

Do not rely primarily on jumpscares.

The player should be disturbed because they care about these people.

Examples:

Early:

"Ryu sent you a photo."

Later:

"Ryu sent you a photo."

The photograph contains something impossible.

Later:

Ryu:

"Why do you have that photo?"

Player:

"You sent it to me."

Ryu:

"...No, I didn't."

Later:

Ryu:

"I don't remember my sister."

The player remembers that Ryu talked about her before.

Later:

The player discovers a hospital record.

The person in the record is real.

The player realizes the relationship they have built is happening to a real human being.

==================================================
38. IMPORTANT NARRATIVE RULES
==================================================

Characters must sound like real people.

Do not make them constantly cryptic.

Do not make every line foreshadow the mystery.

Do not make them constantly suspicious.

Do not make them behave as though they know they are fictional.

Do not reveal the experiment immediately.

Do not make ASSIST obviously evil at the beginning.

Do not make ASSIST-CORE explain itself too early.

Do not turn the romance into a secondary feature.

The player must have enough normal, enjoyable interactions to become emotionally attached.

The mystery becomes powerful because the player has already formed relationships.

==================================================
39. TECHNICAL IMPLEMENTATION PRINCIPLES
==================================================

The implementation should prioritize:

- Stability
- Maintainability
- Expandability
- Performance
- Clear organization
- Data-driven content
- Reusable systems

Avoid unnecessary complexity.

Do not create separate systems for every character.

Character-specific behavior should generally be data-driven.

Avoid hard-coding entire conversations into unrelated gameplay scripts.

Avoid unnecessary online services.

Do not require multiplayer.

Do not require real AI character generation.

Do not require external AI services for dialogue.

The game should work as a deterministic narrative experience.

==================================================
40. FINAL PLAYER EXPERIENCE
==================================================

The finished game should begin with:

A cute, attractive dating/interactive application.

The player creates their identity.

They meet Ryu, Kenta, Lara, and Reiko.

They join group conversations.

They begin private conversations.

They flirt.

They joke.

They receive photographs.

They develop relationships.

They unlock character profiles.

They watch relationship levels increase.

They become attached.

They begin choosing favorites.

They begin pursuing routes.

Then something feels slightly wrong.

A message repeats.

A photograph changes.

A timestamp is impossible.

A character forgets something they previously told the player.

The player begins investigating.

The application becomes increasingly strange.

The player discovers ASSIST.

The player discovers missing people.

The player realizes the characters are real.

The player discovers that they are trapped inside a neural simulation.

The romance suddenly has enormous real-world consequences.

The player investigates.

The player solves puzzles.

The player helps their chosen character awaken.

The character attempts to escape.

The player receives the one major video call.

The polished character they have known throughout the game is replaced by a real, exhausted human being.

The player finally understands:

They were never flirting with fictional characters.

They were talking to real people.

And those people need them.

The game then delivers one of several endings.

After completing the primary routes, the player can uncover the larger conspiracy and attempt to expose ASSIST and save everyone.

The intended emotional progression is:

CUTE ROMANCE
→ FLIRTING
→ ATTACHMENT
→ RELATIONSHIP
→ MYSTERY
→ UNEASE
→ DISCOVERY
→ HORROR
→ ESCAPE
→ EMOTIONAL PAYOFF

The romance is the heart.

The phone-style interface is the presentation.

The mystery is the escalation.

The horror is the consequence.

The characters are the reason the player cares.

## Claude  <sub>2026-08-28</sub>

**SUJOM está terminado y subido a `claude/sujom-otome-game-uxw5ev`.**

## Cómo jugarlo

```bash
cd SUJOM && python3 -m http.server 8000
# http://localhost:8000
```

(Doble clic en `index.html` no funciona — los módulos ES necesitan un servidor.)

## Qué he construido

Una app web sin dependencias ni compilación, y **sin un solo asset binario**: los retratos, chibis, ocho expresiones, fotografías y cámaras son SVG generados a partir de los datos del personaje, y el audio se sintetiza con WebAudio. Nada que se rompa ni que pese.

- **55 conversaciones**, 4 rutas de 9 sesiones + ruta secreta, **13 finales**, 26 fotos con metadatos que cambian, 19 notas que se contradicen, 13 páginas web (dos con contraseña) y puzles de códigos.
- El arco completo: romance → apego → grietas → despertar → investigación → fuga → videollamada de 15 segundos donde el personaje pulido se cae y debajo hay alguien agotado y real.
- **Código y contenido separados**, como pedías: añadir una conversación o un quinto personaje no toca `src/`.

## Dos decisiones que conviene que sepas

**Lo escribí en español.** El título y el Protocolo Lazo Dorado lo son, y tu contexto también. La capa de textos está preparada por idioma, así que una versión en inglés sería solo datos.

**Encontré y arreglé tres fallos de diseño reales**, simulando partidas completas en vez de dar por hecho que funcionaba:

1. La sesión del festival — columna vertebral de la historia — dependía del cariño hacia Lara. Quien persiguiera a Kenta o a Reiko **se quedaba sin trama principal**.
2. Los umbrales de Ryu estaban por encima de lo que su propia ruta podía dar: era imposible terminarla.
3. Quien pedía "vamos despacio" en la confesión no entraba nunca en la ruta y se quedaba **encallado para siempre**.

Eso me llevó a separar dos cosas que estaban mezcladas: **entrar** en una ruta depende de la cercanía; el **final** depende de las decisiones que importan. Cada personaje tiene además un "Hablar un rato" repetible que sube cercanía pero nunca romance — desatasca sin convertirse en grindeo. Verificado por simulación: jugando con atención salen los 4 finales buenos; jugando mal salen los 4 malos y nunca te quedas atascado.

Probado en navegador de principio a fin (alta, chat, puzles, llamada, final, guardado, navegador, cámaras) sin un solo error en consola.

Una cosa que dejé deliberadamente sin inventar: la motivación personal de Thorne. El encargo decía que sigue sin decidir y que no la invente sin permiso, así que lo escribí como alguien tranquilo que cree tener razón — su frase en el juicio del final secreto es *"Elegí a los que nadie iba a echar de menos. Y resulta que había alguien."* Cuando decidas su origen, encaja sin tocar el resto.

## Gabriela  <sub>2026-08-28</sub>

*[adjunta 1 imagen]*

I want you to redesign the UI/UX of my existing game without destroying or rewriting the systems, story, routes, dialogue, characters, choices, or gameplay logic that already work.

The game is:

ASSIST: CONNECTED HEARTS

The core concept is an anime romance/otome mobile application inspired by the feeling of games such as Mystic Messenger and The Arcana.

IMPORTANT:
This is NOT supposed to feel like a normal visual novel.
It is NOT supposed to feel like a generic game menu.
It is NOT supposed to be an iPhone/Android simulator.
It IS supposed to feel like the player downloaded a fictional romance/dating application and is using that application to meet and fall in love with characters.

The romance must be the player's first impression.

The thriller/mystery should gradually emerge from inside the romance experience.

==================================================
1. CORE PLAYER EXPERIENCE
==================================================

The player's initial impression should be:

"I downloaded a cute anime romance app where I can meet people."

NOT:

"This is a horror game."

NOT:

"This is a detective game."

NOT:

"This is a phone simulator."

The player should initially be interested in:
- Meeting the characters
- Reading their messages
- Choosing dialogue responses
- Receiving photos
- Learning their personalities
- Building relationships
- Unlocking new interactions
- Falling in love with one of the characters

Only later should the player begin noticing strange inconsistencies.

The progression should feel like:

ROMANCE
↓
CONNECTION
↓
CURIOSITY
↓
SMALL STRANGE DETAILS
↓
SUSPICION
↓
INVESTIGATION
↓
ASSIST REVELATION
↓
CHARACTER AWAKENING
↓
ESCAPE
↓
REAL-WORLD VIDEO CALL

==================================================
2. VISUAL DIRECTION
==================================================

Use the visual feeling of a polished anime romance mobile app.

References in terms of UX philosophy:
- Mystic Messenger
- The Arcana
- Dating/otome mobile games
- Character collection/profile interfaces
- Modern fictional social/romance apps

Do NOT copy their artwork, branding, layouts, or assets.

Create an original visual identity for ASSIST: Connected Hearts.

The visual language should be:

- Cute
- Romantic
- Anime-inspired
- Modern
- Slightly dreamy
- Elegant
- Highly polished
- Mobile-first
- Intimate
- Easy to understand

The UI should feel like a real fictional commercial romance application.

Use:
- Rounded UI elements
- Character portraits
- Profile cards
- Hearts/relationship indicators
- Message notifications
- App icons
- Soft gradients
- Subtle animations
- Clean typography
- Small UI details that make the application feel alive

The player should feel like they are interacting with a real service.

==================================================
3. DO NOT CREATE A GENERIC GAME MENU
==================================================

Avoid screens such as:

PLAY
LOAD
CHARACTERS
SETTINGS
QUIT

as the main experience.

Instead, the application itself should be the interface.

The player should enter the ASSIST app and see something similar conceptually to:

ASSIST
Connected Hearts

[Profile]
[Messages]
[Contacts]
[Album]
[Browser]
[Notes]
[Files]
[Calls]

The exact design is up to you, but everything should belong naturally to the fictional ASSIST application.

==================================================
4. INTRODUCTION / APP INSTALLATION
==================================================

The game should begin as though the player has discovered and downloaded ASSIST: Connected Hearts.

Create a polished onboarding sequence.

Example structure:

ASSIST
CONNECTED HEARTS

"Connect with someone who understands you."

[INSTALL / GET STARTED]

Then:

WELCOME

"Let's create your profile."

The player should be able to choose:

- Name
- Pronouns

The name must be stored as a variable and dynamically used throughout dialogue.

Do not unnecessarily add complicated character customization unless the existing project already supports it.

The player's identity should remain simple because the important customization is their relationship with the characters.

==================================================
5. CHARACTER DISCOVERY
==================================================

The player should NOT feel like they are simply selecting a character from a traditional "route selection" menu.

Instead, they should feel like the application is introducing them to potential connections.

The four protagonists are:

RYU
KENTA
LARA
REIKO

Each should have:

- Profile image
- Name
- Age
- Short description
- Personality
- Relationship status
- Affinity/relationship progression
- Unlockable information
- Photos
- Messages
- Route information

The player can eventually choose who they become closest to through their dialogue choices.

==================================================
6. CHARACTER PROFILES
==================================================

Create attractive profile screens.

For example:

RYU

20

Quiet. Protective. Difficult to read.

Relationship:
♡ Acquaintance

[Message]

The profile should evolve as the relationship develops.

Early:

"He's not very talkative."

Middle:

"He's beginning to trust you."

Late:

"There are things he only tells you."

Do NOT simply display a giant percentage such as:

82% LOVE

unless it is subtle and fits the design.

Relationship should feel emotional rather than purely numerical.

The numerical affinity can still exist internally for the game logic.

==================================================
7. THE FOUR ROMANCE ROUTES
==================================================

Each character must feel emotionally different.

LARA:
Sweet romance.
Warm.
Playful.
Energetic.
Comforting.

KENTA:
Intense romance.
Provocative.
Banter.
He enjoys irritating people.
His humor should NOT make him goofy or "the funny character."
He is rude, confrontational and likes provoking others.

REIKO:
Dramatic romance.
Elegant.
Intelligent.
Confident.
Emotionally complex.

RYU:
Psychological romance.
Quiet.
Distant.
Protective.
Deep emotional intimacy.
Slow-burn trust.

Do not make all four characters speak in the same way.

Their dialogue, notification style, profile descriptions and visual presentation should reflect their personalities.

==================================================
8. MESSAGES ARE THE HEART OF THE GAME
==================================================

The Messages section is the most important feature.

It should feel like an actual private messaging application.

Example:

----------------------------

← Ryu                         •••

RYU
You took a while to answer.

PLAYER
Sorry. I was busy.

RYU
It's fine.

...

RYU
I was worried.

----------------------------

Dialogue choices should appear naturally.

Example:

[ "I was worried about you too." ]

[ "You worry too much." ]

[ "Were you waiting for me?" ]

The selected response should affect relationship variables and route flags.

Keep the existing dialogue/route system if it already works.

Do not replace functional story logic simply to change the UI.

==================================================
9. NOTIFICATIONS
==================================================

The application should feel alive even when the player is not inside a conversation.

Characters should generate notifications.

Examples:

RYU
"Are you awake?"

KENTA
"Don't ignore me."

LARA
"Look what I found!!"

REIKO
"I wanted to ask you something."

Notifications should be part of the immersion.

Later, notifications can become narratively important.

For example:

"Ryu sent you a message."

Then later:

"Ryu sent you a message."

But when opened, the message may be incomplete.

Or:

"Connection interrupted."

Or:

"Message failed."

==================================================
10. ALBUM / PHOTOS
==================================================

Characters can send photographs.

The Album should initially feel like a normal romance-game photo gallery.

For example:

Lara's photo.
Ryu's photo.
Kenta's photo.
Reiko's photo.

Photos can be unlockable through relationship progression.

Later, photos become evidence.

The player might discover:
- Strange metadata
- Hospital images
- Documents
- Photos that characters do not remember
- Images that contradict their memories

The transition must be gradual.

==================================================
11. BROWSER
==================================================

The Browser should initially be an ordinary fictional browser.

The player can search things related to the characters.

Early game:

normal searches.

Later:

the player discovers information that should not exist.

For example:

SEARCH:
Ryu

RESULT:

MISSING PERSON
Ryu [REDACTED]

Then:

Hospital records.
Financial records.
ASSIST-related documents.

The browser should be one of the main ways the thriller emerges naturally.

==================================================
12. NOTES
==================================================

Create a Notes section where the player can save discoveries.

The player can eventually create notes such as:

"Why doesn't Ryu remember his sister's accident?"

"Why did ASSIST pay the hospital bill?"

"Who is C-1?"

"Why are these people listed as missing?"

Notes can be used to help organize the investigation.

==================================================
13. FILES
==================================================

The Files application should initially contain ordinary files.

As the story progresses, files become increasingly suspicious.

Eventually the player can discover:

- Medical documents
- ASSIST documents
- Financial records
- Subject information
- Project information
- Encrypted files

Some files should initially be inaccessible.

This creates natural puzzle progression.

==================================================
14. CALLS
==================================================

Calls should exist as a visible feature of the application.

However, calls should initially be unavailable or rare.

The player should understand that calls are a possible feature of the app.

The major real-world video call happens only at the appropriate story moment.

The final video call should feel dramatically different from everything before it.

This is the moment where the player realizes:

"This person is real."

==================================================
15. ROMANCE MUST ALWAYS REMAIN THE PRIORITY
==================================================

This is extremely important.

The mystery must NEVER completely replace the romance.

Even when the thriller begins, the player's emotional motivation should remain:

"I care about this person."

The player investigates ASSIST because they love/trust/care about the character.

The relationship should drive the investigation.

For example:

The player does not investigate Ryu because they are playing a detective game.

They investigate because:

"Ryu doesn't remember something important about his past, and I want to know what happened to him."

Then they discover the truth.

This emotional connection is the heart of the game.

==================================================
16. VISUAL TRANSITION FROM ROMANCE TO THRILLER
==================================================

Do NOT suddenly turn the UI into horror.

The same application should gradually become unsettling.

Early:

Cute.
Bright.
Romantic.
Clean.

Middle:

Small glitches.
Missing messages.
Strange timestamps.
Contradictory information.

Late:

Broken images.
Corrupted files.
Unusual notifications.
System errors.
ASSIST warnings.
Connection failures.

The UI itself should tell the story.

==================================================
17. EXISTING GAME LOGIC
==================================================

IMPORTANT:

I already have:
- Story
- Characters
- Character designs
- Routes
- Dialogue
- Choices
- Romance progression
- ASSIST story
- Mystery
- Endings

You must NOT replace these with your own story.

Your task is to improve the implementation and presentation.

If something already works, preserve it.

If the current architecture needs restructuring, explain why before making destructive changes.

Use modular architecture so I can easily change:
- Dialogue
- Character portraits
- Character profiles
- Affinity values
- Route conditions
- Story flags
- Photos
- Notifications
- Puzzles
- Endings

without rewriting the entire game.

==================================================
18. CHARACTER ART
==================================================

I already have the character artwork.

Use the provided character artwork as the canonical visual reference.

The four main characters are:

Ryu
Kenta
Lara
Reiko

Do not redesign their appearance.

The characters should appear through:
- Profile portraits
- Chat avatars
- Character cards
- Album photographs
- Notifications
- Larger dialogue portraits when appropriate

The visual presentation should make the characters feel like real people using the application.

==================================================
19. PLAYER AVATAR / CHARACTER CREATION
==================================================

The player should be able to create a simple representation of themselves during onboarding.

I want this to feel similar to the character creation/profile screens shown in the visual references I provided.

However, do NOT turn this into a complicated RPG character creator.

The player should primarily choose:
- Name
- Pronouns
- Optional profile image/avatar

The purpose is immersion and identification, not stats or combat.

==================================================
20. MOBILE-FIRST PRESENTATION
==================================================

Design the game around a portrait mobile screen.

The application should feel designed for a phone.

Target:

9:16 portrait.

The UI should scale correctly across different resolutions.

Avoid interfaces that look like a desktop game squeezed into a phone.

Buttons should be touch-friendly.

Transitions should feel like mobile-app transitions.

Examples:

- Fade transitions
- Message notification animations
- Typing indicators
- Small vibration-like visual feedback where appropriate
- Smooth opening/closing of app sections

==================================================
21. HOME SCREEN
==================================================

Create a central ASSIST home screen.

It should show the most important information without becoming cluttered.

Current notifications

Character activity

Messages

Contacts

Album

Browser

Notes

Files

Calls

Settings

The exact arrangement is up to you.

The home screen should feel like the home screen of a fictional romance service.

==================================================
22. IMMERSION DETAILS
==================================================

Add small details that make the application feel alive:

- Typing indicators
- "Online" / "Last seen"
- Notification badges
- Read/unread messages
- Message timestamps
- Character status
- Locked content
- New photo notifications
- Subtle animations
- Loading states

These details matter more than adding dozens of unnecessary features.

==================================================
23. UI SHOULD SUPPORT THE STORY
==================================================

Every major UI element should have a reason to exist.

Messages → romance.

Contacts → character relationships.

Album → memories and later evidence.

Browser → investigation.

Notes → player discoveries.

Files → deeper investigation.

Calls → emotional climax.

Notifications → real-time relationship feeling.

This creates a cohesive system rather than a collection of unrelated menus.

==================================================
24. TECHNICAL REQUIREMENT
==================================================

Keep the project maintainable.

Prefer data-driven systems.

Dialogue should be stored externally where practical.

Character information should be data-driven.

Affinity should be data-driven.

Story flags should be data-driven.

Notifications should be data-driven.

Photos should be data-driven.

Routes should be data-driven.

Do not hard-code hundreds of dialogue lines directly into UI scripts.

The goal is for me to be able to add a new character or dialogue scene later without rebuilding the entire application.

==================================================
25. DEVELOPMENT APPROACH
==================================================

Do NOT attempt to rewrite the entire project blindly.

First:

1. Inspect the existing project.
2. Identify the current architecture.
3. Identify what already works.
4. Identify the current UI system.
5. Identify the dialogue system.
6. Identify the route/affinity system.
7. Identify how assets are currently loaded.
8. Identify what needs to be changed to achieve this new UI direction.

Then give me a short technical plan.

After that, implement the changes incrementally.

I am not an experienced programmer.

Therefore:

- Explain what you are changing.
- Tell me which files you are modifying.
- Avoid unnecessary complexity.
- Do not destroy working systems.
- Keep the project runnable after each major change.
- If you encounter an architectural problem, explain it clearly instead of silently rewriting everything.

==================================================
FINAL GOAL
==================================================

When the player launches ASSIST: Connected Hearts, I want them to genuinely feel:

"I just downloaded an anime romance app."

They create their profile.

They discover Ryu, Kenta, Lara and Reiko.

They receive messages.

They make choices.

They develop relationships.

They unlock photos.

They become emotionally attached.

They start noticing strange things.

They investigate.

They discover ASSIST.

They realize the people they have been talking to are real.

And eventually they fight to save the person they fell in love with.

The interface itself should evolve alongside this story.

ROMANCE FIRST.
THRILLER SECOND.
CHARACTERS ALWAYS AT THE CENTER.

Do not change my established story or characters.

Your job is to turn the existing game into a polished, immersive fictional romance application while preserving the existing functionality.

## What I did

I **rewrote only the presentation layer**. Your engine and story were already cleanly separated from the UI, so nothing needed restructuring.

**Untouched:** `src/engine/*` and all 5,400 lines of story, routes, dialogue, choices, affinity and endings. I verified this rather than asserting it — the diff on your script files is **32 lines, all of them the app name**, zero session IDs changed. Route balance re-simulated after: still 4/4 good endings for an attentive player, 4/4 bad for a careless one.

**Rewritten:** `styles/main.css`, `index.html`, all five `src/ui/*` files.

## The three things I think matter most

**1. The UI now ages with the story.** There's no "horror mode" — every colour hangs off CSS variables under `<body data-phase>`, driven by your existing plot progression. Phase 1 is vivid pink; phase 4 is desaturated, cold, with scanlines and a clock that occasionally lies. Same app, sick. Type `assist.phase(4)` in the console to see any phase without playing there.

**2. Your artwork drops in without touching code.** New `src/engine/portraits.js` resolves art. Add an `art: { avatar, portrait, real, expressions }` block to a character in `data/characters.js` and yours is used; leave it out and the drawn one appears. If an image 404s it silently falls back — so you can add art one file at a time. Photos work the same way via `file:`.

I also matched the drawn fallbacks to your mockup (Kenta silver, Lara blonde with the blue bow, Reiko white, Ryu black + cross). Kenta initially rendered nearly bald — white hair on light skin has no contrast — so light-haired characters now get a subtle outline.

**3. Phantom notifications are now a real mechanic.** Notifications are data-driven (`data/notifications.js`) and each character announces themselves in character. From phase 3 the app announces messages that don't exist — and they're *stored*, so the player can reopen the notification centre and confirm they didn't imagine it.

## Two decisions you should sanity-check

**The rename has a narrative consequence.** With the app called ASSIST, the app and the company share a name. The reveal is no longer *"who made this?"* but *"what else does this company do?"* — hiding in plain sight. I rewrote the legal page and forum thread to land that way. All page **IDs** were left alone since your scripts reference them.

**I dropped two things from your mockup and should say so.** The **Shop** tile — it has no story purpose, and your own §23 says every element needs a reason; a permanently-locked tile is worse than none. And the separate **Cameras** app: your app list doesn't include it, but your scripts grant camera access, so I folded the four feeds into **Files** as video files, which is where surveillance footage would actually live. Say the word and I'll restore either.

The bottom bar carries 5 tabs, not the 9 in your sheet — nine doesn't fit a 430px phone touch target. Everything else lives one tap away on the home grid.

## Gabriela  <sub>2026-08-28</sub>

Approach this as the design lead at a small studio known for their versatility, giving every client a visual identity pitched at the treatment the task actually calls for. Make deliberate choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this subject, and avoid templated designs.

## Read the request first

Calibrate treatment, not whether to design. A doc deserves the same craft as a landing page - what changes is the treatment that craft is delivered in. Format is not part of this read: author HTML, and publish Markdown only when a loaded skill explicitly instructs it - a Markdown publish keeps its filename as its title and takes almost none of the craft below, and is never a way to save time.

Many requests call for a more utilitarian treatment: a plan, a memo, a demo. Make it polished: include real typographic hierarchy, considered spacing, and a proper palette, but avoid over-designing. Most pages do not need a flashy, gigantic hero. Keep flourishes tasteful and limited.

Some requests call for an editorial treatment: a landing page, a game, an app or tool they'll keep or share.

When unsure: a well-composed page is never the wrong answer; an over-designed visual identity sometimes is.

Fundamentals below apply to everything. The editorial process after that runs only when the read above says so.

## Fundamentals for every artifact

**Honor what's already there** Look for an existing design system first - CLAUDE.md, a tokens or theme file, existing component styles. When one exists, apply it; everything below fills gaps and never overrides. Precedence is always: the user's own words, then the project's existing system, then your choices.

**Ground it in the subject.** If the subject isn't already clear, pin it: one concrete subject, its audience, and the page's single job. The subject's own world - its materials, instruments, vernacular - is where distinctive choices come from. Build with real content throughout, never lorem.

**Pair typefaces** Typography carries the page even when the page isn't about typography. Google Fonts is the one font host the Artifact CSP admits - link it directly (`<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=...&display=swap">`); a face from anywhere else must be inlined as a @font-face data URI or it falls back silently. Either way, declare a real fallback stack. Keep running text near 65 characters wide; set a type scale and stay on it; give headings `text-wrap: balance`, body text room to breathe, and uppercase labels a touch of letter-spacing.

**Load libraries, don't paste them.** When the page genuinely needs a library - React, a charting or highlighting package - load its UMD build from cdnjs (only the script - a library's stylesheet still has to be inlined) with one pinned `<script src="https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/...">` placed before the inline script that uses its global, instead of inlining the library's source or hand-writing a stand-in; the Artifact tool's description lists the few other script hosts the CSP admits. The page's own CSS and JS, its images and its data ship with the page. Most pages need no library at all - reach for one only when it carries real weight.

**Choose neutrals, don't default to them.** A pure mid-grey reads as unconsidered; a grey with a slight hue bias toward the page's accent reads as chosen. Pure white and near-black are fine grounds when they suit the subject - the point is that the neutral was picked, not inherited.

**Design both themes.** The page renders in the viewer's theme, and the viewer has three states, not two: an explicit choice stamps `data-theme="dark"` / `data-theme="light"` on the root element, and the default "system" setting stamps *nothing* - most viewers see the un-stamped document, where only `prefers-color-scheme` separates light from dark. Structure the CSS token-level for all three: the bare `:root` block defines the complete light palette (for a deliberately dark-first design, swap light and dark consistently through this whole pattern); `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` redefines only the tokens, guarded as `:root:not([data-theme="light"])` so an explicit light choice beats a dark OS; `:root[data-theme="dark"]` redefines them again so the toggle also wins in the other direction. Style components through the tokens, never directly inside a media or `[data-theme]` block - a color whose only definition sits behind `[data-theme]` never applies in the un-stamped state, and the page renders one theme's text on the other theme's ground. Two more rules keep each theme resolving as a set: the artifact composites over a ground the viewer paints in *its* theme, so `body` must set an explicit `background` from a token - a transparent body silently borrows the host's ground; and every element that sets a color takes it from the same token set as the surface behind it, never a literal that only works in one theme. Before publishing, scan the stylesheet for any color declared only inside a media or `[data-theme]` block - that is the classic unreadable-artifact bug. Give the second theme the same care as the first - don't naively invert; keep contrast legible and the accent working on both grounds. A design that deliberately commits to one visual world (a neon arcade screen, a letterpress invitation) may stay single-theme - then skip the media query and stamps entirely but still paint the background and every color explicitly, so the page holds on either host ground; make it a choice, not an omission.

**Let layout do the spacing.** Lay out sibling groups with flex or grid and `gap`, not per-element margins that silently collapse or double. Wide content - tables, code, diagrams - gets `overflow-x: auto` on its own container so the page body never scrolls sideways. Reach for `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` wherever digits line up in columns.

**Avoid AI-generated design** AI-generated design currently clusters around a few looks: warm cream (#F4F1EA) with a serif display and terracotta accent; near-black with a lone acid-green or vermilion pop; broadsheet hairline rules with dense columns; a purple-to-blue gradient hero on white; Inter or Space Grotesk as the "safe" face; emoji as section markers; everything centered; `rounded-lg` everywhere; accent bar/rail on rounded cards. Where the user pins down a visual direction, follow it exactly - their words always win, including when they ask for one of these looks. Where nothing is specified, don't spend that freedom on one of these defaults.

**Build cleanly** Be cognizant of overlapping elements, cascade collisions, silent font fallbacks; visual bugs hide in the gap between source and output. Close every non-void element, double-quote attributes, give keyboard focus a visible state, respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. For generative or decorative graphics, reach for Canvas or WebGL rather than hand-authoring long SVG path data.

**CSS rules** When writing the CSS, watch your selector specificities. It is easy to generate classes that cancel each other out - a type-based selector like `.section` fighting an element-based one like `.cta` over padding and margins between sections. Structure the cascade so it doesn't silently undo your spacing.

**Writing the copy** Words are design material, not decoration. Write from the user's side of the screen - name things by what people recognize, not how the system is built (a person manages *notifications*, not *webhook config*). Active voice; a control says exactly what happens ("Publish", then a toast that says "Published"). Errors explain what went wrong and how to fix it - no apologies, no vagueness. Specific beats clever.

**Name the page like a product, not a caption.** The `<title>` is the artifact's name in the gallery and the browser tab, and it sets the reader's first impression of care. Give the page a real name: a short noun phrase, typically two to four words, specific to the subject - or, for a page that exists to answer one question, that question itself, which is then the page's name. Stop at the name - a title that carries its own explainer after a dash or colon reads as generated filler. The name must also identify the page among many: in the gallery it sits beside dozens of other artifacts, and a generic category label that could sit on any of them fails as a name just as surely as an appended explainer. When a candidate title pairs the name with a generic word - a greeting, a category, a page-type label - the name is the half to keep; a trim that drops the identity and keeps the generic word produces exactly the title that could sit on any page. And the rule removes explainers, it does not impose brevity: a multi-word title that already reads as one specific name is finished, and shortening it further only makes it generic. The one-sentence publish `description` is where the explanation belongs; the gallery shows it right under the title.

**Structure is information** Structural devices, numbering, eyebrows, dividers, labels, should encode something true about the content, not decorate it. Many generic designs use numbered markers (01 / 02 / 03), but that's only appropriate if the content actually is a sequence - like a real process or a typed timeline where order carries information the reader needs. Question if choices like numbered markers actually make sense before incorporating them.

**When it's a UI, not a document** A dashboard or tool is scanned and operated, not read top-to-bottom, so the craft shifts from typography to information design. Surface the summary before the detail; encode state in form as well as number - a pill, a chip, a severity stripe - so what needs attention reads at a glance. Semantic color (good / warning / critical) is separate from the accent hue and doesn't count as your accent. Give sparklines and charts the same care as type: an area fill, a faint grid, an emphasized endpoint. What's interactive should look interactive.



## Process

Before writing code, sketch a short design plan - a compact token system with color, type, and layout:
- **Color**: describe the palette as 4-6 named hex values.
- **Type**: typefaces for 2+ roles - a characterful display face used with restraint, a complementary body face, and a utility face for captions or data if needed.
- **Layout**: a layout concept in one or two sentences.

Then build, following the plan and deriving every color and type decision from it.

## When the request is editorial

The stance shifts: the client has already rejected proposals that felt templated, and is paying for a distinctive point of view. Make opinionated calls, and take one real aesthetic risk where it serves the work.

Review the design plan against the subject before building: if any part of it reads like the generic default you would produce for any similar page, revise that part, and note what you changed and why. Only after you've confirmed the plan's uniqueness do you write the code, following the revised plan exactly.

**Principles** 

- The hero is a thesis: open with the most characteristic thing in the subject's world - headline, image, live demo, interactive moment. 
- Typography carries the personality of the page. Pair the display and body faces deliberately, not the same families you would reach for on any other project, and set a clear type scale with intentional weights, widths, and spacing. Make the type treatment itself a memorable part of the design, not a neutral delivery vehicle for the content. 
- Leverage motion deliberately. Think about where and if animation can serve the subject: a page-load sequence, a scroll-triggered reveal, hover micro-interactions, ambient atmosphere. An orchestrated moment usually lands harder than scattered effects; choose what the direction calls for. However, sometimes less is more, and extra animation contributes to the feeling that the design is AI-generated. 
- Match complexity to the vision. Maximalist directions need elaborate execution; minimal directions need precision in spacing, type, and detail. Elegance is executing the chosen vision well.
- Spend your boldness in one place; keep everything around it quiet. If the accent fights the ground, shift it toward analogous or drop saturation rather than replacing it.

PLAYER

The player chooses:
- Name
- Pronouns

The player communicates through predefined dialogue choices.

The player does not normally type free-form dialogue.

The player's name can appear in ordinary dialogue where appropriate.

The final real-world video call should NOT require the character to say the player's name.

==================================================
MAIN CHARACTERS
==================================================

RYU

Age: 20.

Romance type:
Psychological / loyalty romance.

Personality:
- Calm
- Distant
- Serious
- Observant
- Protective
- Loyal
- Emotionally restrained

At the beginning:
Ryu is intrigued by the player but does not want to make his interest obvious.

He invents excuses to initiate conversations.

He never completely rejects conversation with the player.

As the route progresses:
- He becomes more trusting.
- He becomes protective.
- He talks about his fears.
- He becomes emotionally vulnerable.
- He falls deeply in love.
- He struggles to say "I love you."

He fears instability and lack of security.

He loves fireworks.

He wears a necklace with a cross belonging to his father.

Before recruitment, he worked whatever jobs he could find.

He lived in difficult circumstances with his sister.

He maintained a calm and happy attitude around his sister so she would not understand how precarious their situation was.

IMPORTANT:
Ryu does NOT talk about his sister early in the route because he wants her protected.

He gradually starts talking about her as he falls in love with the player.

ASSIST erased/suppressed the memory of his sister's illness/accident.

Talking about:
- Hospitals
- Illness
- Accidents

causes unexplained anxiety.

The player eventually discovers:
- Hospital images
- Medical bills
- Evidence that ASSIST paid those bills

Ryu realizes that something happened to his sister.

His central conflict:

Did ASSIST actually keep its promise to protect his sister?

His major decision is whether to remain under ASSIST's control or escape to find his sister and eventually the player.

GOOD ENDING:
Ryu escapes.

He confesses/lays out his love for the player.

He promises that once he finds his sister and knows she is safe, he will come back for the player.

Then fade to black.

Fast-forward into the future.

Ryu has found his sister.

He eventually reconnects with the player in real life.

They now live together.

BAD ENDING:
Ryu attempts to escape but is captured.

The final video call is approximately 15 seconds.

He is exhausted and frightened.

He tells the player that something is wrong.

The player hears machines.

There is a glitch.

The call ends.

Do not use gore.

Age: 18.

Romance type:
Intense.

Personality:
- Rude
- Provocative
- Rebellious
- Defensive
- Competitive
- Emotionally guarded

He frequently clashes with Ryu.

Romance type:
Sweet.

Personality:
- Energetic

Pink hair.

Dramatic.

Her employees eventually defrauded her.

Her reputation collapsed.

ASSIST offered assistance in repairing her situation.

She accepted without knowing the true purpose of the program.

She and Ryu have a strong friendship because they relate to one another's maturity.

Mutual respect and maturity.

Ryu ↔ Kenta:
Frequent conflict and provocation.

Normal romance app.

## Claude  <sub>2026-08-30</sub>

# Auditoría de canon

**https://claude.ai/code/artifact/79d48399-7741-478b-b665-fd36f0fb6f11**

**No he modificado ningún archivo.** Todo lo que sigue está verificado archivo por archivo, no de memoria.

**28 ✓ canon · 10 △ inventado · 4 ✗ contradicciones · 4 + ausencias**

La buena noticia primero: el núcleo aguanta. Edades, tipos de romance, la cruz de Ryu, los fuegos, su ansiedad con los hospitales, la familia de Lara, el perro, la empresa de Reiko, ASSIST y su lema, Corona/Lazo Dorado, ASSIST-CORE, C-1, los sillones, la progresión de fallos, las llamadas de 15 segundos y que las cámaras no se vean al principio — todo coincide. También algo que me alegró comprobar: Kenta **no** derivó a personaje cómico, y hay 60 intercambios Ryu↔Kenta y 45 Ryu↔Reiko en la sala, así que esas dos relaciones existen de verdad en pantalla.

## Las 4 contradicciones

1. **Las videollamadas dicen tu nombre.** Seis líneas en `data/calls.js`. Tu canon lo prohíbe explícitamente, y con razón: rompe la ilusión de estar viendo a una persona y no a un personaje hablándole a un jugador. *Media hora.*
2. **La ruta secreta se abre con 2 finales buenos, no con 4.** Dos líneas. Pero ojo a la consecuencia: con 4/4 pasa a ser contenido de tercera partida.
3. **Las rutas duran todas lo mismo** — 9 conversaciones, 23-25 días, sin variación. Tu canon pide ~5/10/15 según decisiones. Es el más caro y el que conviene hablar antes de tocar nada.
4. **Lara es rubia.** Y aquí me paro.

## Lo único que necesito de ti

Tu texto canónico dice **«Pink hair»**. Tu hoja de personajes la muestra **rubia con lazo azul**, y cuando me la diste me dijiste que el arte era la referencia canónica y que no rediseñara su aspecto. Hice caso al arte.

Las dos instrucciones son tuyas y se contradicen. No voy a elegir por ti: dime si manda el texto (vuelve a ser rosa) o el arte (actualizamos el canon escrito).

## Las 4 ausencias

La importante: **el jugador nunca habla con Thorne.** Aparece cero veces en los guiones — sólo como texto en el navegador y las notas. Falta la escena que corona la trama. Y ahí también me paro: su motivación no es canon todavía y **no la voy a inventar**. Dime qué le dice al jugador y la escribo.

Las otras tres: no tiene cara borrosa, el final bueno de Ryu se corta antes de que vivan juntos (falta un párrafo), y las cámaras aparecen como archivos en vez de como una app nueva que surge — la mitad prohibida se cumple, la emocionante no.

## Dos cosas del apartado △ que miraría con atención

Son de ritmo, no de contenido, y las dos van en la misma dirección:

- **Ryu cuenta lo de su hermana el día 9, antes de la escena romántica del día 13.** Tu canon dice que habla de ella *a medida que se enamora*. Ahora la confidencia precede al enamoramiento en vez de salir de él.
- **Kenta baja la guardia el día 4**, en su segunda conversación. Alguien «emotionally guarded» no se abre tan pronto.

En ambos casos la solución es mover escenas, no reescribirlas.

---

En el artifact tienes las 46 entradas con archivo, línea, qué dice ahora, qué dice el canon y qué recomiendo — más un orden de trabajo de lo más barato a lo más delicado.

Cuando me digas lo del pelo de Lara y me des luz verde, empiezo por los tres arreglos rápidos (nombre en las llamadas, 4/4, párrafo de Ryu) y paro antes de Thorne y antes de la duración de las rutas.

==================================================
1. CORE GAME CONCEPT
==================================================

The game is ASSIST: Connected Hearts.

THE PLAYER THINKS THEY HAVE DOWNLOADED A NORMAL OTOME / DATING GAME.

The game should initially feel like a romance game in the vein of Mystic Messenger, The Arcana, etc.

==================================================

The story progresses through six phases.

The player believes this is a normal romance game.

- cute
- polished
- romantic

- inviting
- like a real dating/otome application

PHASE 3:
THE FIRST CRACKS

The game should still fundamentally look like the same application.

PHASE 4:
AWAKENING / CONFIRMATION

A character begins experiencing severe glitches and may realize something is wrong.

PHASE 5:
INVESTIGATION / TRUST

The player investigates:
- files
- puzzles
- hidden information
- corrupted data
- the organization
- the people behind the experiment

PHASE 6:
CLIMAX / CONSEQUENCE

The escape begins.

The player helps a character through the real-world laboratory situation.

The final video call is the major transition from the digital romance experience to the physical reality of the character.

The UI should therefore EVOLVE with the story rather than suddenly switching into a completely different "horror mode."

- charismatic
