# Artifact runtime capabilities

A published Artifact page can declare **runtime capabilities** — abilities the claude.ai viewer grants the page at open time — by passing `capabilities: {name: config}` to the Artifact tool. The control plane is the authority on valid names and config shapes. Declaration gestures: **omitting** `capabilities` on a redeploy carries the stored declaration forward unchanged (and preserves the artifact's stored contract pin); an **empty object** `{}` is the explicit clear-all; a **non-empty object** is a full-set declaration (anything stored but not restated is revoked). Moving a republished artifact's runtime version is a deliberate gesture — pass `contract: 'latest'` to upgrade, or a specific version to pin or roll back — never a side effect of editing.

**Available capabilities:** `artifact`, `db`, `downloads`, `mcp`, `room`, `sample`, `self` — the complete set of capability names you may declare. Anything not listed is unavailable to this user.

Runtime contract 0.2.41


Capability namespaces live behind `claude.use(name)`: `const db = await claude.use("db")` resolves the capability's namespace, or `null` when this view cannot run it (not served, not granted, or failed to load — indistinguishable by design). Branch on `null` and design for absence. `window.claude` carries only `use`: no `window.claude.db`, `.room`, or `.artifact` member is ever promised, so never read one — render the page without them and light features up when the promise resolves (later, never within your script's first run, and unordered with DOMContentLoaded; `null` after 10 s when no viewer answers). The resolved namespace is frozen and platform-owned: call its functions and keep the reference; never assign to it, `defineProperty` on it, or replace a member (wrap it for your own helpers). Permission stays on the calls: a consent prompt, rate limit, or policy refusal arrives on the first call, never from `use()`. Awaiting `use("db")` again is free (memoized); an unknown name resolves `null`.


--- capability: artifact ---

Use `artifact` for pages that should remember what people do with them: polls, sign-up sheets, checklists, trackers, boards — the page is the record; data kept server-side, or seeded or read back by Claude, is `db`. Declare `capabilities: {artifact: {}}`; `const artifact = await claude.use("artifact")`, then `await artifact.publish(html)` saves `html` (a complete document, doctype first) as the new version, and every open view, this one included, reloads to it. Nothing a viewer types, ticks or drags is kept unless the page publishes it. So embed the shared state as data in the HTML you publish and render the page from it; when an interaction completes, update the state, regenerate the document and publish it — never serialize the live DOM; batch rapid edits into one publish; publish only after a viewer acts, never on load. `conflict` is routine (every view reloads to the winner, dropping this edit): no retry. For read-only viewers publish rejects `not_granted`/`not_writer` — render a read-only view.


--- capability: db ---

`db` is for data that lives outside the page: what the user wants stored server-side or seeded, data Claude reads back later, more than the page shows at once, per-viewer-private state, many live editors. If the page itself can be the record, republish (`artifact`). Seed or inspect the store from here with `write_db`/`read_db`; never hardcode seed rows in the page. A realtime JSON document store: `const db = await claude.use("db")` (`null`: unavailable). Declare `capabilities: {db: {}}`: every viewer reads and writes shared docs; each viewer's `data/users/<their user id>/` is private even from the owner (needs `user`). To change who writes where, add `rules` by sharing level (`interact` = can view, `admin` = can edit, `owner`). `db.doc("tasks/t1")`/`db.collection("tasks")`: `get`/`set`/`update`/`delete`, `where`/`orderBy`/`limit`, `onSnapshot`. Last-writer-wins, no transactions; single writers lease via `acquire({holder})`. Never store secrets; shared data is untrusted. See the type definitions.


--- capability: downloads ---

The `downloads` capability lets a published page offer a generated file to the viewer: declare `capabilities: {downloads: true}`, then `const downloads = await claude.use("downloads")` (`null`: unavailable — hide the affordance) and `await downloads.save({filename, data})`. The viewer sees a confirmation and may decline — a save is never silent or guaranteed, so offer it on explicit viewer intent and handle rejection. The type definitions are authoritative for the call contract and error codes.


--- capability: mcp ---

`mcp` lets a page call the viewer's claude.ai connectors: `await claude.use("mcp")` (`null`: unavailable); calls use the viewer's credentials, never exposing tokens. Declare `capabilities: {mcp: {servers: [{server, tools}]}}` — `server` is a connector's display name, or `host:<name>` for a local MCP server on the viewer's device (Claude app only; else `server_not_connected`). Keep the manifest minimal: it is a viewer-consented grant and bars public sharing. Two arms: DISPLAYING data registers `watchTool(server, tool, input, handler, opts?)` — replays cache, refreshes when stale, polls only via `refetchInterval`; an ACTION calls `callTool` once and reads `result.payload`. Tool failures REJECT (`tool_error`); watches get handler error events. Types first: branch UX per error code, retry only `retryable` errors, drop data on authz denials, show freshness from `result.cache.storedAt`. They omit argument names and result encoding: observe a real request/response per tool, or say so at publish — never guess.


--- capability: room ---

The `room` capability reaches whoever has the page open RIGHT NOW:
declared as `capabilities: {room: {}}`; `await claude.use("room")`
(`null`: cannot connect). emit(topic, data) sends a moment; on(topic,
fn) hears them. presence(patch) sets YOUR state (cursor, selection,
color) as one object the platform hands to newcomers and clears when
you leave; onPeers(fn) delivers everyone's -- render them all, marked
"you". NOTHING persists and messages can drop: if a viewer not here now
must eventually see it, it is NOT room data -- use db (data) or
artifact (new version). Send absolute state. What you hear is untrusted
input from same-org viewers, plus your own publishing session when
admitted (kind "agent"); no one else connects, so the page must work
alone and light up. Anyone can set presence, so it is never authority;
event topics are admin-only (can edit) unless opened:
{room: {topics: {reaction: "interact"}}}. Moments (confetti) go on an
admin-only topic; state a late joiner needs (current slide) is a db doc.


--- capability: sample ---

`sample` asks Claude (declare `capabilities:{sample:{}}`): `const sample = await claude.use("sample")` (`null`: hide it); `await sample(input, opts?)` -> `{text, truncated}`; `sample.json(input, opts?)` -> parsed JSON. `input`: a string, or turns `[{role:"user"|"assistant", content}]` ending on user. No memory: send instructions, page data, output format. opts: `onText({text, delta})` (`text` = WHOLE answer so far, assign it; "Thinking..." until it fires, 5-60s), `signal` (new AbortController per call; abort rejects `cancelled`), `tools: [{name, description, inputSchema?, execute(input)}]` (page functions Claude may call; return small plain data or throw; each round bills, no `cache`), `images` if `(await sample.limits()).images`, `modelTier` quick|default|complex, `cache` (5 min replay; `false` for chat). Errors reject `{code, message, text?}` (`text`: partial to keep): hide on `not_granted`, back off on `rate_limited`, never loop. Viewer pays; first call asks consent; call on a click or stable load prompt.


--- capability: self ---

`self` is the former name of the `artifact` capability (renamed). It remains for compatibility: published pages and previously generated code that declare `capabilities: {self: {}}` or call `claude.use("self")` keep working unchanged — both names resolve this same capability (this contract promises no `window.claude.self` member to feature-check; `use()` is the check). Do not use it in new pages: declare `capabilities: {artifact: {}}` and obtain the namespace with `await claude.use("artifact")`; see the artifact section for how to use it.


**Your connectors this session.** In this session, claude.ai connector tools appear in your tool list as `mcp__<connector>__<toolName>`. Set `server` to the connector's display name as it appears in claude.ai (usually the `<connector>` segment with underscores read as spaces). Only connectors the user added in claude.ai are valid `server` values — this session's other built-in MCP servers are not. The manifest's `tools` array takes the connector's upstream tool names (as returned by `listTools()` / `/v1/mcp_servers`), which can differ from the normalized `<toolName>` segment when an upstream name contains `.` or spaces. Every `servers[]` entry needs a non-empty `tools` array naming the tools the page calls — an empty or omitted `tools` list is refused and never means "all tools"; to publish without connector access, leave `mcp` out of `capabilities` (pass `capabilities: {}` to clear a stored declaration) rather than declaring an empty `servers` list. In hermetic/CI sessions where connectors aren't loaded but `$CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is set, fetch the list via Bash: `curl -H 'anthropic-version: 2023-06-01' -H 'anthropic-beta: mcp-servers-2025-12-04' -H "Authorization: Bearer $CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN" https://api.anthropic.com/v1/mcp_servers?limit=1000`; in that case use each entry's `display_name` as the `server` value (exact display names are always accepted alongside tool-prefix segments).

**Call contract** (runtime contract 0.2.41). The platform-served `window.claude` type definitions for this contract are extracted under `/tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/256bb39043de7915ea08e3e9381fb11d/artifact-capabilities`: `0.2.41/artifact.d.ts`, `0.2.41/claude.d.ts`, `0.2.41/db.d.ts`, `0.2.41/downloads.d.ts`, `0.2.41/mcp.d.ts`, `0.2.41/room.d.ts`, `0.2.41/sample.d.ts`, `0.2.41/self.d.ts`. Read `/tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/256bb39043de7915ea08e3e9381fb11d/artifact-capabilities/0.2.41/claude.d.ts` (how a page reaches any capability on this contract) and `/tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/256bb39043de7915ea08e3e9381fb11d/artifact-capabilities/0.2.41/mcp.d.ts` before writing any code that calls the `mcp` capability — they are authoritative for this contract version over any remembered API shape. The type definitions cover only the call envelope — they do not tell you a connector tool's argument names or its result encoding. Never publish a page that calls a connector tool without having observed one real request/response pair for that tool in this session; if you cannot safely observe one (for example, the connector is unauthenticated here, or calling the tool would have side effects), say that explicitly to the user at publish time — in your reply, not as a note inside the published page — instead of shipping a guessed shape. Observed response payloads are the user's real data: learn the shape from them, but never embed the observed values in the published page as sample or placeholder data.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-06</sub>

**Direcciones visuales de ASSIST** → https://claude.ai/code/artifact/24e7e9d8-cca2-4038-a1a0-0dc1a77f71b3

Dos filas, las mismas seis pantallas en las dos (alta, inicio, mensajes, conversación, ficha, videollamada) más una tira de las cuatro fases al final de cada una. Puedes compararlas en vertical: cada columna es la misma pantalla en las dos direcciones.

**A · Caramelo** — la app arranca **clara**: blanco, rosa fuerte, esquinas muy redondeadas, tipografía cálida. Parece un otome gratuito e inofensivo. La gracia está en la tira de fases: como empieza tan luminosa, cuando se apaga hay una caída enorme. El coste es que las fases oscuras hay que construirlas de cero.

**B · Medianoche** — oscura y elegante desde el minuto uno, serif con dorado, radios más cerrados, todo en minúscula y más seco. Es la que encaja con Ryu de 26 años y con la Reiko sensual del canon. El coste está en su propia tira de fases: **casi no hay caída**. Si la app ya es oscura el día uno, el terror tiene que venir del comportamiento —el reloj que miente, la foto repetida— y no del color.

Ése es el verdadero eje entre las dos, y por eso te he puesto las fases debajo de cada una: no estás eligiendo un color, estás eligiendo cuánta distancia quieres que caiga.

Dos cosas que asumí, dime si me paso:

- Son **maquetas estáticas**, para mirar. No son jugables.
- Los personajes salen con **el canon ya corregido** — Lara rosa y de 18, Kenta naranja y de 23, Ryu 26, el perro se llama Sandwich. Sólo en estas maquetas: **el juego sigue sin tocar**. Los retratos son marcadores geométricos, no un intento de imitar tu arte.

Dale una vuelta mientras le doy un segundo repaso; si veo algo descuadrado lo arreglo.

const p = await b.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1500, height: 950 }, deviceScaleFactor: 2 });
const rows = [];
for (const n of names) {
  await p.goto('file://' + dir + n + '.html');
  await p.waitForTimeout(700);
  const m = await p.evaluate(() =&gt; {
    const el = document.body.firstElementChild;
    const walk = (node) =&gt; {
      let worst = 0, who = '';
      for (const c of node.querySelectorAll('*')) {
        const o = c.scrollHeight - c.clientHeight;
        if (c.clientHeight &amp;&amp; o &gt; worst) { worst = o; who = (c.getAttribute('style')||'').slice(0,60); }
      }
      return { worst, who };
    };
    return { w: el.offsetWidth, h: el.offsetHeight,
             sh: el.scrollHeight, ...walk(el) };
  });
  rows.push({ n, ...m });
  await p.locator('body &gt; *').first().screenshot({ path: dir + n + '.png' });
}
await b.close();
for (const r of rows) {
  const over = r.sh - r.h;
  console.log(
    (over &gt; 1 ? 'DESBORDA ' : 'ok       ') +
    r.n.padEnd(20) + `${r.w}x${r.h}` .padEnd(11) +
    (over &gt; 1 ? `+${over}px` : '') +
    (r.worst &gt; 1 ? `  interno +${r.worst}px  ${r.who}` : ''));
}

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-06</sub>

Repaso terminado y lienzo actualizado — mismo enlace: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/24e7e9d8-cca2-4038-a1a0-0dc1a77f71b3

Encontré cinco cosas y las arreglé todas:

- **Las dos tiras de fases se cortaban.** Declaré el marco a 1376 px pero el relleno se sumaba por fuera, así que se comían 80 px de ancho. Ahora las 14 pantallas miden exactamente lo que dicen medir.
- **El retrato de la ficha salía rebanado** por el borde del panel de abajo. Ahora el círculo monta por encima, que era la intención.
- **Hueco muerto en el inicio y en las minipantallas.** Enseñaba seis casillas cuando tu app tiene nueve. Ahora salen las nueve reales, incluidas Llamadas, Finales y Ajustes.
- **Un fallo de coherencia mío**, y es el que más me molesta: en Medianoche, Ryu salía con el pelo azul claro. Estaba usando su color de acento como color de pelo. Su pelo es negro en las dos direcciones; el azul se queda sólo para texto e iconos.
- Los ojos de los retratos de marcador se leían como fosas nasales. Retocados.

Lo demás aguantó. Las doce pantallas de móvil encajan en 390×844 sin recortar nada.

Sigo sin tocar el juego. Cuando quieras, retomamos las respuestas del canon donde las dejaste.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-06</sub>

Mismo enlace: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/24e7e9d8-cca2-4038-a1a0-0dc1a77f71b3

Cuatro filas, y en las cuatro las mismas tres pantallas: **entrada · conversación · personaje**. Lo que cambia entre filas no es el color, es qué clase de aplicación es.

**A · Deck** — una app de citas de verdad. Tarjeta a pantalla completa, botones de pasar y me gusta, ficha con preguntas tipo Hinge. Encaja con tu canon mejor que ninguna: ASSIST te sirve a gente que ya había elegido por ti, y tú crees que eliges. La pega: la sala común de los cuatro no tiene sitio natural.

**B · Bandeja** — sólo mensajes. **No hay pantalla de inicio ni cuadrícula de iconos**: la app abre directamente en la lista de conversaciones, dos pestañas y se acabó. La ficha es una hoja que sube con datos secos, casi de ajustes. Es la que más creíble hace «esto es gente escribiéndome», y también la más fría: todo el romance tendría que salir del texto.

**C · Cuaderno** — un diario. Papel, cartas con lacre, fotos con cinta, letra a mano. Los mensajes no son burbujas sino bloques de carta, y las opciones son tiras de papel. Las pestañas de abajo son las de un cuaderno, no una barra de app. Es la más bonita y la que menos parece un móvil.

**D · Idol** — juego de colección. Cartas con rareza, niveles de vínculo, energía, banner de evento, misiones diarias, y las opciones te enseñan cuánta afinidad dan. Hace legible tu sistema de relaciones; el precio es que enseñar los números rompe un poco la ilusión de que son personas.

Dos avisos:

- Los huecos grandes de ilustración van marcados como **«ilustración pendiente»** a propósito. No quiero fingir un dibujo donde va el tuyo.

Base directory for this skill: /tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/518cd1154f23dfccca25095af6aa7103/artifact-capabilities

# Artifact runtime capabilities

A published Artifact page can declare **runtime capabilities** — abilities the claude.ai viewer grants the page at open time — by passing `capabilities: {name: config}` to the Artifact tool. The control plane is the authority on valid names and config shapes. Declaration gestures: **omitting** `capabilities` on a redeploy carries the stored declaration forward unchanged (and preserves the artifact's stored contract pin); an **empty object** `{}` is the explicit clear-all; a **non-empty object** is a full-set declaration (anything stored but not restated is revoked). Moving a republished artifact's runtime version is a deliberate gesture — pass `contract: 'latest'` to upgrade, or a specific version to pin or roll back — never a side effect of editing.

**Available capabilities:** `artifact`, `db`, `downloads`, `mcp`, `room`, `sample`, `self` — the complete set of capability names you may declare. Anything not listed is unavailable to this user.

Runtime contract 0.2.41


Capability namespaces live behind `claude.use(name)`: `const db = await claude.use("db")` resolves the capability's namespace, or `null` when this view cannot run it (not served, not granted, or failed to load — indistinguishable by design). Branch on `null` and design for absence. `window.claude` carries only `use`: no `window.claude.db`, `.room`, or `.artifact` member is ever promised, so never read one — render the page without them and light features up when the promise resolves (later, never within your script's first run, and unordered with DOMContentLoaded; `null` after 10 s when no viewer answers). The resolved namespace is frozen and platform-owned: call its functions and keep the reference; never assign to it, `defineProperty` on it, or replace a member (wrap it for your own helpers). Permission stays on the calls: a consent prompt, rate limit, or policy refusal arrives on the first call, never from `use()`. Awaiting `use("db")` again is free (memoized); an unknown name resolves `null`.


--- capability: artifact ---

Use `artifact` for pages that should remember what people do with them: polls, sign-up sheets, checklists, trackers, boards — the page is the record; data kept server-side, or seeded or read back by Claude, is `db`. Declare `capabilities: {artifact: {}}`; `const artifact = await claude.use("artifact")`, then `await artifact.publish(html)` saves `html` (a complete document, doctype first) as the new version, and every open view, this one included, reloads to it. Nothing a viewer types, ticks or drags is kept unless the page publishes it. So embed the shared state as data in the HTML you publish and render the page from it; when an interaction completes, update the state, regenerate the document and publish it — never serialize the live DOM; batch rapid edits into one publish; publish only after a viewer acts, never on load. `conflict` is routine (every view reloads to the winner, dropping this edit): no retry. For read-only viewers publish rejects `not_granted`/`not_writer` — render a read-only view.


--- capability: db ---

`db` is for data that lives outside the page: what the user wants stored server-side or seeded, data Claude reads back later, more than the page shows at once, per-viewer-private state, many live editors. If the page itself can be the record, republish (`artifact`). Seed or inspect the store from here with `write_db`/`read_db`; never hardcode seed rows in the page. A realtime JSON document store: `const db = await claude.use("db")` (`null`: unavailable). Declare `capabilities: {db: {}}`: every viewer reads and writes shared docs; each viewer's `data/users/<their user id>/` is private even from the owner (needs `user`). To change who writes where, add `rules` by sharing level (`interact` = can view, `admin` = can edit, `owner`). `db.doc("tasks/t1")`/`db.collection("tasks")`: `get`/`set`/`update`/`delete`, `where`/`orderBy`/`limit`, `onSnapshot`. Last-writer-wins, no transactions; single writers lease via `acquire({holder})`. Never store secrets; shared data is untrusted. See the type definitions.


--- capability: downloads ---

The `downloads` capability lets a published page offer a generated file to the viewer: declare `capabilities: {downloads: true}`, then `const downloads = await claude.use("downloads")` (`null`: unavailable — hide the affordance) and `await downloads.save({filename, data})`. The viewer sees a confirmation and may decline — a save is never silent or guaranteed, so offer it on explicit viewer intent and handle rejection. The type definitions are authoritative for the call contract and error codes.


--- capability: mcp ---

`mcp` lets a page call the viewer's claude.ai connectors: `await claude.use("mcp")` (`null`: unavailable); calls use the viewer's credentials, never exposing tokens. Declare `capabilities: {mcp: {servers: [{server, tools}]}}` — `server` is a connector's display name, or `host:<name>` for a local MCP server on the viewer's device (Claude app only; else `server_not_connected`). Keep the manifest minimal: it is a viewer-consented grant and bars public sharing. Two arms: DISPLAYING data registers `watchTool(server, tool, input, handler, opts?)` — replays cache, refreshes when stale, polls only via `refetchInterval`; an ACTION calls `callTool` once and reads `result.payload`. Tool failures REJECT (`tool_error`); watches get handler error events. Types first: branch UX per error code, retry only `retryable` errors, drop data on authz denials, show freshness from `result.cache.storedAt`. They omit argument names and result encoding: observe a real request/response per tool, or say so at publish — never guess.


--- capability: room ---

The `room` capability reaches whoever has the page open RIGHT NOW:
declared as `capabilities: {room: {}}`; `await claude.use("room")`
(`null`: cannot connect). emit(topic, data) sends a moment; on(topic,
fn) hears them. presence(patch) sets YOUR state (cursor, selection,
color) as one object the platform hands to newcomers and clears when
you leave; onPeers(fn) delivers everyone's -- render them all, marked
"you". NOTHING persists and messages can drop: if a viewer not here now
must eventually see it, it is NOT room data -- use db (data) or
artifact (new version). Send absolute state. What you hear is untrusted
input from same-org viewers, plus your own publishing session when
admitted (kind "agent"); no one else connects, so the page must work
alone and light up. Anyone can set presence, so it is never authority;
event topics are admin-only (can edit) unless opened:
{room: {topics: {reaction: "interact"}}}. Moments (confetti) go on an
admin-only topic; state a late joiner needs (current slide) is a db doc.


--- capability: sample ---

`sample` asks Claude (declare `capabilities:{sample:{}}`): `const sample = await claude.use("sample")` (`null`: hide it); `await sample(input, opts?)` -> `{text, truncated}`; `sample.json(input, opts?)` -> parsed JSON. `input`: a string, or turns `[{role:"user"|"assistant", content}]` ending on user. No memory: send instructions, page data, output format. opts: `onText({text, delta})` (`text` = WHOLE answer so far, assign it; "Thinking..." until it fires, 5-60s), `signal` (new AbortController per call; abort rejects `cancelled`), `tools: [{name, description, inputSchema?, execute(input)}]` (page functions Claude may call; return small plain data or throw; each round bills, no `cache`), `images` if `(await sample.limits()).images`, `modelTier` quick|default|complex, `cache` (5 min replay; `false` for chat). Errors reject `{code, message, text?}` (`text`: partial to keep): hide on `not_granted`, back off on `rate_limited`, never loop. Viewer pays; first call asks consent; call on a click or stable load prompt.


--- capability: self ---

`self` is the former name of the `artifact` capability (renamed). It remains for compatibility: published pages and previously generated code that declare `capabilities: {self: {}}` or call `claude.use("self")` keep working unchanged — both names resolve this same capability (this contract promises no `window.claude.self` member to feature-check; `use()` is the check). Do not use it in new pages: declare `capabilities: {artifact: {}}` and obtain the namespace with `await claude.use("artifact")`; see the artifact section for how to use it.


**Your connectors this session.** In this session, claude.ai connector tools appear in your tool list as `mcp__<connector>__<toolName>`. Set `server` to the connector's display name as it appears in claude.ai (usually the `<connector>` segment with underscores read as spaces). Only connectors the user added in claude.ai are valid `server` values — this session's other built-in MCP servers are not. The manifest's `tools` array takes the connector's upstream tool names (as returned by `listTools()` / `/v1/mcp_servers`), which can differ from the normalized `<toolName>` segment when an upstream name contains `.` or spaces. Every `servers[]` entry needs a non-empty `tools` array naming the tools the page calls — an empty or omitted `tools` list is refused and never means "all tools"; to publish without connector access, leave `mcp` out of `capabilities` (pass `capabilities: {}` to clear a stored declaration) rather than declaring an empty `servers` list. In hermetic/CI sessions where connectors aren't loaded but `$CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is set, fetch the list via Bash: `curl -H 'anthropic-version: 2023-06-01' -H 'anthropic-beta: mcp-servers-2025-12-04' -H "Authorization: Bearer $CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN" https://api.anthropic.com/v1/mcp_servers?limit=1000`; in that case use each entry's `display_name` as the `server` value (exact display names are always accepted alongside tool-prefix segments).

**Call contract** (runtime contract 0.2.41). The platform-served `window.claude` type definitions for this contract are extracted under `/tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/518cd1154f23dfccca25095af6aa7103/artifact-capabilities`: `0.2.41/artifact.d.ts`, `0.2.41/claude.d.ts`, `0.2.41/db.d.ts`, `0.2.41/downloads.d.ts`, `0.2.41/mcp.d.ts`, `0.2.41/room.d.ts`, `0.2.41/sample.d.ts`, `0.2.41/self.d.ts`. Read `/tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/518cd1154f23dfccca25095af6aa7103/artifact-capabilities/0.2.41/claude.d.ts` (how a page reaches any capability on this contract) and `/tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.263/518cd1154f23dfccca25095af6aa7103/artifact-capabilities/0.2.41/mcp.d.ts` before writing any code that calls the `mcp` capability — they are authoritative for this contract version over any remembered API shape. The type definitions cover only the call envelope — they do not tell you a connector tool's argument names or its result encoding. Never publish a page that calls a connector tool without having observed one real request/response pair for that tool in this session; if you cannot safely observe one (for example, the connector is unauthenticated here, or calling the tool would have side effects), say that explicitly to the user at publish time — in your reply, not as a note inside the published page — instead of shipping a guessed shape. Observed response payloads are the user's real data: learn the shape from them, but never embed the observed values in the published page as sample or placeholder data.

**[Dulce, Arcana y Sala](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/2729b294-c383-4b75-bdc0-c44d389d3b37)** — tres direcciones, cuatro pantallas cada una (inicio · conversación · personaje · una cuarta propia):

- **A · Dulce** — pastel, redondito, corazones. Linaje Corazón de Melón: portada con episodio nuevo, carrusel de vínculos, burbujas con sombra suave, opciones que te dicen cuánto afecto dan, lista de episodios con estrellas y candados. Es la más inofensiva de las tres, que es exactamente el punto: nadie sospecha de una app rosa.
- **B · Arcana** — ciruela y oro, cartas de tarot, serif elegante. Cada personaje es un arcano: Ryu es IX El Ermitaño, Lara es XVII La Estrella. El vínculo son fases lunares. «Alguien pensó en ti antes de que abrieras la aplicación» es una frase de horóscopo *y* una amenaza, y la app la vende como lo primero.
- **C · Sala** — el chat de grupo en vivo, que es la referencia que me enseñaste. La pantalla estrella no es el DM: es la sala común donde los cuatro hablan entre ellos y tú llegas a mitad de conversación. Reiko: «Yo llevo aquí desde antes, solo estaba mirando.»

Dos decisiones que cambié a propósito: los retratos ahora son siluetas —marcan dónde entra tu ilustración sin fingir una cara que no es la tuya— y cada pantalla tiene diálogo escrito para ese personaje, no la conversación de prueba que repetí en las tandas anteriores.

Lara (18) rosa largo, **ojos rosas**, piel morena clara, lazo negro, ahoge. Reiko **25** —la tenía en 24— gris claro y ojos casi blancos. Y el canon se cuela en la interfaz: en la ficha de Lara, «Cómo se llama de verdad» sale bloqueado, porque Lara no es su nombre real.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. **Primary Request and Intent:**

The user is designing the UI for **ASSIST: Connected Hearts**, an otome game where the player unknowingly chats with people whose consciousness has been uploaded into an app. Across the conversation the intent narrowed through repeated rejections into a clear target:

- Show **full UI**, not just chat screens.
- The app must read as a **genuine otome game** (Mystic Messenger / Corazón de Melón / The Arcana lineage) — "la persona descargándose esta aplicación es con el [objetivo] de jugar un juego otome". The surface must be charming and marketable; the horror lives underneath.
- It must have **personality/soul** and be **innocent** ("Con personalidad. Inocente"), imitating a phone interface like Mystic Messenger **but unique**.
- Use the **canon character designs** (from the user's character sheets), with **two registers**: chibi/cute version in mini profile pics and chat, and the "real" illustrated version in photos/CGs.
- Most recent and binding correction: the dark "night mode" I built was rejected — *"Da miedo en el mal sentido. Parece un thriller con muñecos malos en vez de un juego otome. estas son las referencias que quiero usar. **No las vuelvas oscuras.** Simplemente mantén los que las hace especiales y que se adapten a nuestra app"*.

2. **Key Technical Concepts:**
- Claude Design canvas skill: `.dc.html` artboards + `canvas.json` layout, seeded via `seed-canvas.mjs` into `payload.template.html`, published with the Artifact tool.
- Publishing contract: always `contract: "0.1.31"`; first publish declares `capabilities: {"self": {}, "downloads": {}}`; `favicon` required on first publish; republish to the same file path keeps the URL.
- Python-generated HTML/SVG pipeline: `gen.py` → `preview.py` → `measureN.mjs` (Playwright/chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194/chrome-linux/chrome`) for screenshots + 390×844 overflow checking.
- **SVG sticker-halo trick**: define the figure once in `<defs><g id="fUID">`, then two `<use>` — first `stroke="#fff" stroke-width="7"`, then `stroke="{LINE}" stroke-width="2"`. Inner shapes must not set their own `stroke` (they inherit); non-outlined shapes set `stroke="none"`.
- Two-register character art: `chibi()` (big head, dot eyes, blush) vs `bust()` (adult proportions, almond eyes, shoulders).
- Expression system: face = eyes + mouth + optional mark + blush multiplier.
- Cozy-sticker CSS idiom: thick outline + **solid** offset shadow (`box-shadow: 0 4px 0 …`, never blurred) + per-corner irregular `border-radius`.
- Gold-ornament idiom: double gold border with corner scroll (`curl`), ribbons, crown, sakura watermarks, cloud-shaped stickers.
- **Python <3.12 f-strings cannot contain backslashes in expression parts** — must extract fragments into local variables.

3. **Files and Code Sections:**

- **`scratchpad/canvas7/art.py`** (copied into canvas8 and canvas9) — the character art module, the most reusable asset produced.
  - `CANON` dict with per-character hair/skin/cloth/wall/blush/eye colours and canon ages.
  - `_sticker(uid, body, vb, w, h, halo=7, lw=2, halo_col=HALO)` — the double-`<use>` halo trick.
  - `HAIR_CHIBI` / `HAIR_REAL` dicts with `back`/`front`/`over`/`side` path strings per character.
  - `chibi(who, h, uid, sleepy=False, cara=None, halo=True)` and `bust(who, h, uid, halo=False)`.
  - Expression system appended at the end:
```python
FACES = {
  'normal':   ('abiertos', 'sonrisa',  None,      None,        1.0),
  'feliz':    ('felices',  'risa',     None,      'destello',  1.25),
  'guino':    ('guino',    'abierta',  None,      'destello',  1.15),
  'sonrojo':  ('abiertos', 'pequena',  'worried', 'corazon',   1.5),
  'apuro':    ('abiertos', 'tembleque','worried', 'gota',      1.35),
  'enfado':   ('abiertos', 'morro',    'angry',   'enfado',    1.1),
  'sorpresa': ('grandes',  'abierta',  None,      None,        1.0),
  'dormido':  ('cerrados', 'pequena',  None,      None,        1.0),
}
def face_chibi(who, cara):  # -> (svg, blush_multiplier)
```

- **`scratchpad/canvas8/orn.py`** — the gold ornament kit derived from the user's pink/gold references.
```python
P = dict(fonts='…Playfair+Display…Cormorant+Garamond…Nunito…',
  fh="'Playfair Display',Georgia,serif", fb="'Nunito',system-ui,sans-serif",
  fi="'Cormorant Garamond',Georgia,serif",
  bg='#fbe8e2', bg2='#f7dbd3', panel='#fffcfa', bubble='#fffdfc', mine='#f9dce0',
  gold='#cfa143', gold2='#e9c886', gold3='#f6e8cb',
  lila='#ded4f1', lila2='#b5a2de', lila3='#f0ebfb',
  ink='#6d4b41', ink2='#9d7c6e', ink3='#c7ada0',
  rose='#ef8ba0', rose2='#f7b8c4', green='#68c37c', line='#e6c9bd')
```
  Functions: `curl()`, `gold_frame(inner, pad, r, bg, corners, extra)`, `ribbon(text, fill, col, size)`, `crown(s)`, `sakura()/petals()`, `sp_name(name)`, `CLOUD` path constant, `cloud_sticker(chibi_svg, w, h, ring, crop=.72)`, `hearts_row()`, `ic()`, `divider(text)`.

- **`scratchpad/canvas8/gen.py`** — the live deliverable. Screens: `mensajes()`, `chat()` (just rewritten), `perfil()`, `casita()`, `stickers()`. Shared: `phone()`, `doc()`, `statusbar()`, `avatar()`, `navbar()`, `_bow()`, `heart_pill()`.
  - `navbar()` reserves 26px padding-top so the floating central heart doesn't overflow the artboard (this fixed a +20px overflow on three screens).
  - The **newly rewritten `chat()`** implements the Haru reference: real illustration as chat background fused to pink, "Kenta te envió un sticker ✿" pill, cloud sticker, corner chibi with a small speech bubble, dotted heart, write-bar, and a labelled circular action row:
```python
    fondo_ilus = (
      f'<div style="position:absolute;left:-26px;top:0;width:290px;height:330px;'
      f'overflow:hidden;pointer-events:none">'
      f'<div style="display:flex;justify-content:center">{bust(who, 330, "chbg")}</div>'
      f'<div style="position:absolute;inset:0;background:'
      f'linear-gradient(to right,transparent 24%,{P["bg"]} 92%),'
      f'linear-gradient(to bottom,transparent 46%,{P["bg"]} 94%)"></div></div>')
```
  and the action row iterating `[('corazón',…), ('cara',…), ('reacción',…), ('Kenta', avatar(...)), ('regalo',…)]`.

- **`scratchpad/canvas6/gen.py`** — "La Casita" cozy-sticker kit (thick `#8a6350` outlines, solid offset shadows, hand-drawn status bar, 2×2 room grid). Still the source of the "casita" metaphor.

- **Canon reference sheets read**: `scratchpad/ryu-p1.png`, `kenta-p1.png`, `lara-p1.png`, `reiko-p1.png`.

4. **Errors and fixes:**

- **`ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 16`** in `mix()` — caused by 3-digit hex shorthand (`'#fff'`) and later by `rgba(...)` values. Fixed by replacing shorthand with 6-digit hex globally, and adding a guard: `if not a.startswith('#') or not b.startswith('#'): return 'rgba(160,120,100,.25)'`.
- **`SyntaxError: f-string expression part cannot include a backslash`** — hit three separate times (canvas5 tabs, canvas8 tabrow, canvas9 chat thread). Fixed each time by extracting the HTML into a local variable before the f-string, e.g. `tabs_box = '<div style="display:flex;gap:4px">' + tabs + '</div>'`.
- **`IndexError` on `ROWY[r]`** in canvas3 annotations — used row index 4 with a 4-element list; fixed by changing to 0 and adding a `NOTE_X` dict.
- **Artboards overflowing 390×844** — repeatedly diagnosed with `measureN.mjs`. Fixes: reserving space for the floating nav heart (26px padding-top), trimming chat exchanges, reducing photo heights, shrinking item tiles.
- **Portrait framing bugs** — smooth `tall()`/`bust()` SVGs cropped into giant floating heads at wide aspect ratios. Fixed by parameterising `bust(who, h, uid, w, fig, align)` so the figure scales relative to frame and can sit off-centre like real key art.
- **Kenta's hair read as a crown** — because the spikes were a separate path and the line stroke drew a seam across the forehead. Fixed by merging spikes and fringe into **one silhouette path**.
- **Lara/Reiko read as nuns/veils** — back hair panels too wide and vertical; fixed by narrowing, tapering and shortening the side locks.
- **Cloud stickers looked empty** — chibi too small inside the blob. Fixed by adding a `crop=.72` parameter that clips the chibi to a bust inside the cloud, and enlarging the chibi.
- **User feedback that changed direction (in order):**
  1. *"I really don't like them"* — rejected Pixel/Hub.
  2. *"no me llaman… falta personalidad/soul"* → answered "Ambas por igual" (atmosphere AND voice) and "Dirección nueva".
  3. *"Eso pienso que es muy dark"* — SEÑAL abandoned; must feel like an otome.
  4. *"No me llaman… Con personalidad. Inocente"* — led to La Casita cozy style.
  5. *"No me termina de convencer. Recuerdas los diseños canon?"* — must use canon designs and the chibi/real dual register.
  6. **Most recent:** *"Es que no me gusta. Da miedo en el mal sentido… No las vuelvas oscuras."* — night mode deleted entirely.

- **Reiko** — plata, jersey de cuello alto crema, falda marino.

La trama de tu propia línea hace de sombreado y encima vuelve la línea entintada en marrón cálido, con una luz suave cayendo de arriba. Ya no es un boceto lavado: es una ilustración que ocupa media pantalla en el perfil y entra en el chat como el Haru de tu referencia.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-07</sub>

Tienes razón, y la crítica es exacta. Voy a analizarlas de verdad antes de tocar nada.

Lo que tienen tus referencias y lo mío no:

1. **Ningún contenedor es un rectángulo.** Son **cartuchos**: el borde se abomba en el centro de cada lado, se corta en diagonal en las esquinas y se enrosca en voluta. Yo dibujo `border-radius` y le pego una voluta *al lado*, que no es lo mismo: en las tuyas la voluta **es** la línea del marco, nace de ella.
2. **El oro es metálico.** Va de crema a ocre a crema a lo largo del trazo. El mío es un color plano.
3. **Hay volumen.** Luz arriba dentro del marco, sombra cálida abajo a la derecha, grano de papel. Lo mío son rellenos planos con una línea de 1px.
4. **Todo se solapa y cuelga.** La cinta monta sobre la ficha, el medallón cuelga de la barra con su remate, hay guirnaldas y colgantes con cadena. Lo mío está apilado en filas que no se tocan.
5. **Los bordes llevan cuentas.** Rosarios de puntitos y rombos en los puntos medios de cada lado.

Lo ataco por donde toca: un sistema de marco ornamental de verdad, con `border-image`, para que el adorno no se estire cuando la ficha crece.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-07</sub>

Publicado, y esta vez el cambio está en el marco.

🔗 https://claude.ai/code/artifact/97e24b79-26d0-4667-a1a2-7e7b4a83f46d

**Tus bocetos, fuera de toda la app.** No queda ninguno. En su sitio hay camafeos: un busto en silueta sobre el tono de cada personaje, dentro de una hornacina con arco. No finge ser arte terminado, pero tampoco deja un hueco feo. Cuando llegue el arte oficial se cambia el camafeo por la ilustración y no hay que tocar nada más — el hueco, el marco y el recorte ya están puestos.

**Y el marco ya no es una caja.** Antes yo dibujaba un `border-radius` y le pegaba una voluta al lado. Ahora cada ficha lleva un cartucho hecho con `border-image`: dibujo el marco una vez en SVG y el navegador coloca las cuatro esquinas **sin deformarlas** y repite el lado. Por eso el adorno se ve igual en una píldora de 40px que en un panel de 400px.

Lo que lleva ese marco, pieza a pieza:

- La esquina se **corta en diagonal** y la línea se enrosca en voluta. La voluta **nace** de la línea, no está apoyada al lado.
- Rombo de remate sobre el corte, con su brillo dentro.
- Doble filete paralelo y, entre ellos, un rosario de cuentas.
- Rombo en el punto medio de cada lado.
- El oro es **metálico**: va de crema a ocre a crema a lo largo del trazo, no es un color plano.

Y el volumen que también faltaba: luz interior arriba, sombra cálida por dentro abajo, sombra proyectada fuera y un grano de papel finísimo encima. Eso es lo que le quita el relleno plano de CSS.

El marco viene en tres colores — oro para lo general, lila para los paneles de personaje, rosa para lo afectivo — y las diez pantallas están montadas encima.

La muñeca de papel del creador de OC y del vestidor sí sigue dibujada por mí, y creo que debe seguir así: ese personaje es modular por definición, no una ilustración fija.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-07</sub>

Voy a parar y decirte lo que creo que pasa de verdad, porque llevo varias rondas diciendo "ahora sí" y no es verdad.

**Tus referencias son ilustraciones de una app, no una app.** Están pintadas: el marco, el icono, el personaje, el fondo y hasta las letras salen del mismo pincel, con textura, luz e irregularidad de mano. Lo mío es HTML y SVG de verdad: formas exactas, rellenos planos, degradados calculados. Puedo cargarlo de adorno —y lo he hecho— pero sigue leyéndose como plástico, porque **es** plástico. El problema no es cuánto adorno le pongo; es el material.

Eso significa que la ronda 9 de "ahora le añado más volutas" no va a cerrar la distancia. Te estaría haciendo perder el tiempo.

Lo que sí funciona son dos caminos distintos:

**A. Tú generas el arte, yo monto la app encima.** Tus referencias salen de un generador de imágenes; yo aquí no tengo ninguno. Si me pasas las pantallas pintadas como las que me enseñas, yo las corto y las convierto en interfaz de verdad: zonas táctiles, estados, scroll, el texto vivo encima, las elecciones funcionando. Ahí sí tendrías el look exacto **y** una app usable. Es el camino que de verdad te lleva a lo que quieres.

**B. Mis maquetas dejan de intentar ser bonitas y pasan a ser el plano.** Sirven para lo que un HTML sí hace bien: inventario de pantallas, flujos, estados, textos, mecánicas, medidas. Y ese plano se lo das a quien pinte —tú o quien contrates— para que sepa exactamente qué hay que dibujar y a qué tamaño.

Si quieres que lo intente **una vez más** en HTML, lo único que me queda por probar de verdad es pasar *todo* —cada borde, cada icono, cada burbuja, no solo las fichas del tablero— por un filtro de deformación para que ninguna línea sea geométricamente limpia. Mejoraría, pero no llegaría a tus referencias, y prefiero decírtelo antes que después.

¿Cuál de los tres?

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-07</sub>

Comprobado: en esta sesión no hay ninguna herramienta de generación de imágenes. Y la respuesta corta a tu pregunta es **no: Claude no pinta.**

La distinción importa:

- **Claude ve imágenes.** Por eso puedo mirar tus referencias y analizarlas, decirte que el marco es un cartucho con voluta y cuentas, o que el oro va de crema a ocre. Eso es visión, entrada.
- **Claude no produce imágenes.** Es un modelo de texto y código. No hay un pincel dentro. Lo que sí hace es **escribir código que dibuja**: SVG, HTML, CSS, Python con PIL. Eso es exactamente lo que llevo doce pantallas haciendo, y por eso te sale plástico — porque son formas geométricas calculadas, no pintura.

Es la diferencia entre alguien que sabe describirte un cuadro con precisión y alguien que sabe pintarlo. Yo soy lo primero.

Tus referencias no salen de aquí. Salen de un **generador de imágenes** — Midjourney, DALL·E, el de Gemini, Stable Diffusion, lo que uses — o de una ilustradora. Esos modelos sí pintan píxeles: por eso tienen textura, luz real, letras hechas a mano y esa irregularidad que a mí no me sale.

Un matiz para que no te sorprenda después: hay entornos donde a Claude se le conecta una herramienta externa de imagen, y entonces sí puede pedirle un dibujo a ese generador. Pero eso es un conector, no Claude; y aquí no hay ninguno — acabo de mirarlo.

Así que el reparto realista es este: **tú (o tu generador) pones la pintura, yo pongo la app.** Tú me pasas las pantallas pintadas en el estilo que ya consigues, y yo las convierto en interfaz de verdad: recorto las piezas, monto las zonas táctiles, los estados, el scroll, el texto vivo encima, las elecciones funcionando. Eso sí te lo puedo dar bien, y es lo que tus imágenes no son todavía — porque son bonitas, pero no se pueden tocar.

¿Te paso una lista de qué pantallas necesito pintadas y en qué medidas, para que se las pidas a tu generador?

## Gabriela  <sub>2026-09-07</sub>

Approach this as the design lead at a small studio known for their versatility, giving every client a visual identity pitched at the treatment the task actually calls for. Make deliberate choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this subject, and avoid templated designs.

## Read the request first

Calibrate treatment, not whether to design. A doc deserves the same craft as a landing page - what changes is the treatment that craft is delivered in. Format is not part of this read: author HTML, and publish Markdown only when a loaded skill explicitly instructs it - a Markdown publish keeps its filename as its title and takes almost none of the craft below, and is never a way to save time.

Many requests call for a more utilitarian treatment: a plan, a memo, a demo. Make it polished: include real typographic hierarchy, considered spacing, and a proper palette, but avoid over-designing. Most pages do not need a flashy, gigantic hero. Keep flourishes tasteful and limited.

Some requests call for an editorial treatment: a landing page, a game, an app or tool they'll keep or share.

When unsure: a well-composed page is never the wrong answer; an over-designed visual identity sometimes is.

Fundamentals below apply to everything. The editorial process after that runs only when the read above says so.

## Fundamentals for every artifact

**Honor what's already there** Look for an existing design system first - CLAUDE.md, a tokens or theme file, existing component styles. When one exists, apply it; everything below fills gaps and never overrides. Precedence is always: the user's own words, then the project's existing system, then your choices.

**Ground it in the subject.** If the subject isn't already clear, pin it: one concrete subject, its audience, and the page's single job. The subject's own world - its materials, instruments, vernacular - is where distinctive choices come from. Whatever the treatment, carry at least one detail only this subject would have - its real units and scales, its document conventions, its terms of art - as content, not ornament; it costs a plain page nothing. Build with real content throughout, never lorem.

**Pair typefaces** Typography carries the page even when the page isn't about typography. Google Fonts is the one font host the Artifact CSP admits - link it directly (`<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=...&display=swap">`); a face from anywhere else must be inlined as a @font-face data URI or it falls back silently. Either way, declare a real fallback stack. Keep running text near 65 characters wide; set a type scale and stay on it; give headings `text-wrap: balance`, body text room to breathe, and uppercase labels a touch of letter-spacing.

**Load libraries, don't paste them.** When the page genuinely needs a library - React, a charting or highlighting package - load its UMD build from cdnjs (only the script - a library's stylesheet still has to be inlined) with one pinned `<script src="https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/...">` placed before the inline script that uses its global, instead of inlining the library's source or hand-writing a stand-in; the Artifact tool's description lists the few other script hosts the CSP admits. The page's own CSS and JS, its images and its data ship with the page. Most pages need no library at all - reach for one only when it carries real weight.

**Choose neutrals, don't default to them.** A pure mid-grey reads as unconsidered; a grey with a slight hue bias toward the page's accent reads as chosen. Pure white and near-black are fine grounds when they suit the subject - the point is that the neutral was picked, not inherited.

**Design both themes.** The page renders in the viewer's theme, and the viewer has three states, not two: an explicit choice stamps `data-theme="dark"` / `data-theme="light"` on the root element, and the default "system" setting stamps *nothing* - most viewers see the un-stamped document, where only `prefers-color-scheme` separates light from dark. Structure the CSS token-level for all three: the bare `:root` block defines the complete light palette (for a deliberately dark-first design, swap light and dark consistently through this whole pattern); `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` redefines only the tokens, guarded as `:root:not([data-theme="light"])` so an explicit light choice beats a dark OS; `:root[data-theme="dark"]` redefines them again so the toggle also wins in the other direction. Style components through the tokens, never directly inside a media or `[data-theme]` block - a color whose only definition sits behind `[data-theme]` never applies in the un-stamped state, and the page renders one theme's text on the other theme's ground. Two more rules keep each theme resolving as a set: the artifact composites over a ground the viewer paints in *its* theme, so `body` must set an explicit `background` from a token - a transparent body silently borrows the host's ground; and every element that sets a color takes it from the same token set as the surface behind it, never a literal that only works in one theme. Declare every token in the bare `:root` block before any media or `[data-theme]` block redefines it - a color that exists only inside one of those blocks is the classic unreadable-artifact bug. Give the second theme the same care as the first - don't naively invert; keep contrast legible and the accent working on both grounds. A design that deliberately commits to one visual world (a neon arcade screen, a letterpress invitation) may stay single-theme - then skip the media query and stamps entirely but still paint the background and every color explicitly, so the page holds on either host ground; make it a choice, not an omission.

**Let layout do the spacing.** Lay out sibling groups with flex or grid and `gap`, not per-element margins that silently collapse or double. Wide content - tables, code, diagrams - gets `overflow-x: auto` on its own container so the page body never scrolls sideways. Reach for `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` wherever digits line up in columns.

**Compose repeated things as one object.** Cards in a row, label/value pairs down a list, badges on siblings: same edges, baselines and inner padding from one to the next, and a recurring element sits in the same place on each. Let content set a container's height and pick a column count the items fill, so nothing stretches over dead space or sits alone in a row. Text that can outgrow its track wraps or scrolls in its own container; clipped text is a bug.

**Not everything is a card.** Border, fill, radius and shadow each say "separate object" - spend them by role, lifting the one thing that needs it, instead of one radius and one shadow stamped on every block, which flattens the hierarchy. Lead with big-number tiles only when those figures are the point of the page.

**Draw charts to the scale.** One scale places marks, ticks and labels, and every label names a value the chart reaches; chart text takes its color from the theme tokens so it reads in both themes; marks, labels and edges stay clear of one another and inside the drawing's bounds - in SVG, leave room in the viewBox for the outermost labels and give every drawn shape an explicit fill.

**Show the page at rest.** Everything meant to be read is visible once the page has loaded, without scrolling to trigger it - that first still frame is what a thumbnail, a shared link, and a skimming reader all get. A section may animate in, but from a visible resting state, never parked at `opacity: 0` waiting on an observer. Size a hero to what it holds, not to the viewport; a `100vh` opener pushes the page itself out of that first frame. A tool or app opens in a realistic working state - the user's real data where it exists, otherwise example rows, a loaded sample, a form someone plausibly filled, plainly marked as examples and never passed off as the user's own figures - so the first look shows what it does; an empty shell waiting for input shows nothing.

**Avoid AI-generated design** AI-generated design currently clusters around a few looks: warm cream (#F4F1EA) with a serif display and terracotta accent; near-black with a lone acid-green or vermilion pop; broadsheet hairline rules with dense columns; a purple-to-blue gradient hero on white; Inter or Space Grotesk as the "safe" face; emoji as section markers; everything centered; `rounded-lg` everywhere; accent bar/rail on rounded cards. Where the user pins down a visual direction, follow it exactly - their words always win, including when they ask for one of these looks. Where nothing is specified, don't spend that freedom on one of these defaults.

**Build cleanly** Be cognizant of overlapping elements, cascade collisions, silent font fallbacks. Close every non-void element, double-quote attributes, give keyboard focus a visible state, respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. For generative or decorative graphics, reach for Canvas or WebGL rather than hand-authoring long SVG path data.

**CSS rules** When writing the CSS, watch your selector specificities. It is easy to generate classes that cancel each other out - a type-based selector like `.section` fighting an element-based one like `.cta` over padding and margins between sections. Structure the cascade so it doesn't silently undo your spacing.

**Writing the copy** Words are design material, not decoration. Write from the user's side of the screen - name things by what people recognize, not how the system is built (a person manages *notifications*, not *webhook config*). Active voice; a control says exactly what happens ("Publish", then a toast that says "Published"). Errors explain what went wrong and how to fix it - no apologies, no vagueness. Specific beats clever.

**Name the page like a product, not a caption.** The `<title>` is the artifact's name in the gallery and the browser tab, and it sets the reader's first impression of care. Give the page a real name: a short noun phrase, typically two to four words, specific to the subject - or, for a page that exists to answer one question, that question itself, which is then the page's name. Stop at the name - a title that carries its own explainer after a dash or colon reads as generated filler. The name must also identify the page among many: in the gallery it sits beside dozens of other artifacts, and a generic category label that could sit on any of them fails as a name just as surely as an appended explainer. When a candidate title pairs the name with a generic word - a greeting, a category, a page-type label - the name is the half to keep; a trim that drops the identity and keeps the generic word produces exactly the title that could sit on any page. And the rule removes explainers, it does not impose brevity: a multi-word title that already reads as one specific name is finished, and shortening it further only makes it generic. The one-sentence publish `description` is where the explanation belongs; the gallery shows it right under the title.

**Structure is information** Structural devices, numbering, eyebrows, dividers, labels, should encode something true about the content, not decorate it. Many generic designs use numbered markers (01 / 02 / 03), but that's only appropriate if the content actually is a sequence - like a real process or a typed timeline where order carries information the reader needs. Question if choices like numbered markers actually make sense before incorporating them.

**When it's a UI, not a document** A dashboard or tool is scanned and operated, not read top-to-bottom, so the craft shifts from typography to information design. Surface the summary before the detail; encode state in form as well as number - a pill, a chip, a severity stripe - so what needs attention reads at a glance. Semantic color (good / warning / critical) is separate from the accent hue and doesn't count as your accent. Give sparklines and charts the same care as type: an area fill, a faint grid, an emphasized endpoint. What's interactive should look interactive.



## Process

Start with what the viewer should be able to do on the page, not only what they will read: if it should take input, keep what people change for whoever opens it next, show live data, or ask Claude something, load the `artifact-capabilities` skill now and design around what it makes available to this user; a page that is only read needs none of that.

Before writing code, sketch a short design plan - a compact token system with color, type, and layout:
- **Color**: describe the palette as 4-6 named hex values.
- **Type**: typefaces for 2+ roles - a characterful display face used with restraint, a complementary body face, and a utility face for captions or data if needed.
- **Layout**: a layout concept in one or two sentences.

Then build, following the plan and deriving every color and type decision from it.

**Write, look once, publish.** Before publishing you may look at the rendered page once - one screenshot of the local file, or the Artifact tool's preview where it offers one - then one pass of edits for what it shows, without a second look. For a page that charts real numbers, take that look rather than skip it, and spend it on the chart. Don't build a test loop around your own file: no repeated screenshots, no pulling the script out to run it through node, no scripts that probe the DOM. That loop spends the session re-checking what a careful write already settled, while the user waits for a link. Then publish, check once any `window.claude` call the preview couldn't run, and stop: the live page is the review surface, and further polish is the user's to ask for. If the user reports something visibly broken - a clipped column, unreadable text, a control that does nothing - fix that and republish once.

## When the request is editorial

The stance shifts: the client has already rejected proposals that felt templated, and is paying for a distinctive point of view. Make opinionated calls, and take one real aesthetic risk where it serves the work.

Review the design plan against the subject before building: if any part of it reads like the generic default you would produce for any similar page, revise that part, and note what you changed and why. Only after you've confirmed the plan's uniqueness do you write the code, following the revised plan exactly.

**Principles** 

- The hero is a thesis: open with the most characteristic thing in the subject's world - headline, image, live demo, interactive moment. 
- Typography carries the personality of the page. Pair the display and body faces deliberately, not the same families you would reach for on any other project, and set a clear type scale with intentional weights, widths, and spacing. Make the type treatment itself a memorable part of the design, not a neutral delivery vehicle for the content. 
- Leverage motion deliberately. Think about where and if animation can serve the subject: a page-load sequence, hover micro-interactions, ambient atmosphere. An orchestrated moment usually lands harder than scattered effects; choose what the direction calls for. However, sometimes less is more, and extra animation contributes to the feeling that the design is AI-generated. 
- Match complexity to the vision. Maximalist directions need elaborate execution; minimal directions need precision in spacing, type, and detail. Elegance is executing the chosen vision well.
- Spend your boldness in one place; keep everything around it quiet. If the accent fights the ground, shift it toward analogous or drop saturation rather than replacing it.

**52 piezas, 7 familias.** Cada ficha lleva medida, nombre de archivo y el prompt listo — con un botón de copiar, para que no tengas que seleccionar texto a mano.

Las tres reglas de arriba son las importantes:

1. **Sin texto dentro de la imagen.** Un nombre pintado no se puede cambiar ni traducir, y los generadores escriben mal. El texto lo pongo yo encima, vivo.
2. **Una cosa por imagen, sobre fondo liso.** Una pantalla entera generada de golpe es bonita de mirar y no sirve: no se puede tocar por partes. Personaje solo, icono solo, marco solo.
3. **Respeta las zonas muertas.** 130 px arriba y 110 px abajo se los comen las barras del móvil. Nada importante ahí.

Hay dos prompts base —piel de día y piel de noche— que van delante de todo lo demás, para que las 52 piezas parezcan del mismo juego.

Y una que quiero destacarte: **D1, el marco dorado.** Pídelo cuadrado, centrado y **vacío por dentro**. Yo lo corto en nueve trozos —cuatro esquinas, cuatro lados, el centro— de modo que las esquinas nunca se deforman y los lados se repiten. Con esa única imagen tengo el marco de toda la app, desde una píldora de 40 px hasta un panel de 400. Eso solo ya se lleva por delante el noventa por ciento de lo que te parecía plástico.

An SVG tile is drawn once; the browser places the four corners undistorted and repeats the sides, so a volute looks identical on a 40px pill and a 400px panel. **Critical rule: the tile must be drawn at 1:1 scale with the border width** (slice 22 → 22px border); drawing large and shrinking makes the ornament vanish.
- **Data-URI SVG in inline `style` attributes must use `url('...')` with single quotes** — double quotes terminate the HTML attribute.
- **CSS paint order gotcha**: a `position:absolute` div *with a background* paints above non-positioned static in-flow siblings regardless of DOM order. Fixed by moving page gradients onto the flex container itself.
- **PIL region-based colourisation** (`ImageDraw.floodfill`) — used to colour line art by closed regions; later abandoned per the "no uses mis bocetos" instruction.
- **Margin-colour gutter detection** for slicing composite sheets: sample the sheet's outer border colour, then find rows/columns where >88% of pixels match it.

```python
ORO = ('#f7e6bb', '#c2963f', '#fbf2da')
ROSA = ('#fde0e8', '#e07b9c', '#fdf0f4')
LILA = ('#ece4fa', '#8f76c6', '#f6f1fd')
C = 22          # esquina, en unidades = px
E = 56          # tramo de lado que se repite
TOT = C * 2 + E

def marco(col=ORO, uid='o', grosor=C):
    clave = (uid, grosor)
    if clave not in _CACHE:
        k = grosor / C
        _CACHE[clave] = (f'border:{grosor}px solid transparent;'
                         f"border-image:url('{_uri(marco_svg(col, uid, k))}') "
                         f'{round(C * k, 2)} / {grosor}px / 0 round;')
    return _CACHE[clave]

def relieve(base='#fffdf8', base2='#fdf3ee'):

            f'radial-gradient(120% 80% at 50% -10%,#ffffff 0%,rgba(255,255,255,0) 60%),'
            f'linear-gradient(168deg,{base},{base2});'

            f'0 -8px 16px -10px rgba(180,130,110,.35) inset,'
            f'0 5px 14px rgba(184,140,128,.18);')
```

- **La regla de las capas** arriba del todo: fondo sin letras / personaje en PNG recortado / texto lo pongo yo. Es exactamente el problema del punto 1.
- **El bloque de estilo** sacado de tus cuatro imágenes: línea fina marrón sin contorno negro, paleta crema y rosa palo de contraste bajo, sombreado suave con pliegues pintados, sonrojo en la nariz. Es lo que los mantiene reconocibles como el mismo mundo. No hace falta pegarlo: los botones ya copian el prompt entero con estilo y negativo.
- **Ficha cerrada de cada uno** con su referencia al lado — pelo, ojos, prenda, accesorio, gesto base. Escribes el nombre arriba y se renombran los archivos de toda la hoja.
- **12 piezas por personaje**: retrato, busto de chat, avatar, seis gestos (neutral, sonrisa, sonrojo, enfado, tristeza, sorpresa), fondo de llamada y dos CG.
- **17 piezas de piel** — papel, marco, burbujas, barra, ocho iconos, botones, cinta, cenefa. Eliges la piel con los chips y los prompts cambian de paleta solos.
- **Empieza por aquí**: nueve piezas con las que monto una pantalla de chat entera y real, donde el texto sea texto de verdad. Si eso sale, el resto es repetir.

Les puse nombres provisionales (Rubio, Rosa, Plata, Moreno) porque no me los has dicho — cámbialos en la hoja y se propagan. Y en las descripciones no uso nombres en los prompts, solo rasgos, porque el generador no sabe quién es "Kenta".

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-08</sub>

Aquí lo tienes.

**Frase que va delante de cada petición, con la imagen adjunta:**

```
Use the attached image as the exact reference. Same character, same face,
same hair, same clothes, same colours, same line style. Do not redesign
anything. Output a PNG with a fully transparent background, nothing behind
the character. No text, no letters, no numbers, no frame, no watermark.
```

**Luego, una pieza por mensaje (adjuntando su imagen):**

| archivo | medida | qué pides |
|---|---|---|
| busto | 512×512 | head and shoulders, facing the viewer, neutral expression |
| avatar | 256×256 | close-up of the face, centred, margin around the head |
| gestos ×6 | 600×600 | head and shoulders: neutral / smiling / blushing and looking away / frowning / sad with eyes down / surprised with eyes wide |
| cuerpo | 960×2080 | full body from the knees up, same pose as the reference |
| llamada | 1170×2532 | full body standing, character in the upper two thirds, bottom third empty |
| cg ×2 | 1560×1170 | horizontal scene, character off-centre |

**Para la interfaz, adjuntando una de tus cuatro láminas:**

```
Use the attached screen as the exact reference for style and palette. Copy
the paper texture, the ornaments and the colours. Output a PNG with a
transparent background. No text, no letters, no numbers.
```

| archivo | medida | qué pides |
|---|---|---|
| papel | 1170×2532 | the background only — no icons, no characters, no text, centre left empty |
| cabecera | 1170×220 | the top ribbon, empty inside |
| barra | 1170×280 | the bottom tray, no icons inside |
| iconos ×8 | 256×256 | one icon per image: envelope, phone, photo album, notebook, folder, globe, shop, star |
| burbujas ×2 | 480×240 | empty speech bubble, tail on the left / tail on the right |
| marco | 360×360 | empty ornamental square frame, hollow centre |
| botón | 480×140 | empty rounded pill button |
| corazones ×3 | 128×128 | one small heart: full / half / empty |

Tres cosas que suelen ser el fallo: pide **una pieza por mensaje** (si le pides seis a la vez te hace una lámina y se inventa el diseño), **vuelve a adjuntar la imagen cada vez** (muchos generadores la olvidan al segundo mensaje), y si no sabe hacer transparencia pídelo **sobre blanco liso** y lo recorto yo.

## Interfaz — 8 prompts

Aquí sube como referencia **una de tus cuatro láminas de pantallas**, no un personaje.

**1 · Papel de fondo** — 9:16
```
A vertical phone wallpaper of decorated paper, in exactly the same art style, texture and colour palette as the reference image. Soft paper grain, faint scattered stars and tiny hearts, a delicate ornamental border around the edges, and the whole centre left empty and calm. No icons, no characters, no buttons, no text of any kind.
```

**2 · Cinta de cabecera** — 4:1 horizontal
```
An empty decorative horizontal ribbon banner with folded tails, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. Soft painted fill, thin hand-drawn outline, hollow centre, flat front view, centred with empty margin around it. Completely empty inside, nothing written on it. Transparent background.
```

**3 · Barra inferior** — 4:1 horizontal
```
An empty horizontal tray or shelf for a phone navigation bar, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. Rounded top corners, a small ornament in the middle, soft painted fill, thin hand-drawn outline. Completely empty, no icons and no text inside. Transparent background.
```

**4 · Iconos** — 1:1, uno por generación
```
A single hand-painted app icon of AN ENVELOPE, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. Rounded square badge with a soft painted symbol inside, thick soft outline, centred, generous empty margin. One icon only, no text, no label. Transparent background.
```
Repite cambiando las mayúsculas por: `A TELEPHONE HANDSET`, `A PHOTO ALBUM`, `A NOTEBOOK AND PENCIL`, `A FOLDER`, `A GLOBE`, `A SHOP AWNING`, `A STAR BADGE`.

**5 · Burbujas** — 2:1, dos generaciones
```
An empty rounded speech bubble with a small tail on the LEFT side, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. Soft painted fill, thin slightly wobbly hand-drawn outline, completely empty inside. One bubble only, nothing written in it. Transparent background.
```
Repite cambiando `LEFT` por `RIGHT`.

**6 · Marco de tarjeta** — 1:1
```
An empty ornamental square frame, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. Ornate corners with small volutes and beads, plain simple straight sides, hollow empty centre, perfectly symmetrical, flat front view. Nothing inside the frame, no text. Transparent background.
```

**7 · Botón** — 3:1 horizontal
```
An empty rounded pill-shaped button, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. Soft painted fill, thin decorative outline, a small ornament at each end. Completely empty, nothing written on it. One button only. Transparent background.
```

**8 · Corazones** — 1:1, tres generaciones
```
A single small painted heart, FULL and solid pink, glossy with a soft highlight and a thin outline, in exactly the same art style and colour palette as the reference image. One heart only, centred, no text. Transparent background.
```
Repite cambiando `FULL and solid pink` por `HALF filled, the left half pink and the right half pale and empty` y luego por `EMPTY, only the outline, no fill`.

---

Si el modelo que uses no saca transparencia, cambia `Transparent background, nothing behind the character` por `Plain solid white background, nothing else in the image` y lo recorto yo.

```
claude mcp add --transport http openart https://mcp.openart.ai/mcp

y reinicias.

La ventaja no es solo que salga mejor — es que **no puede desviarse nunca**. El mismo código, la misma paleta, los cuatro personajes y las cincuenta piezas. ## Claude  <sub>2026-09-08</sub>

Ahí lo tienes. Un solo relleno partiendo del pecho pinta el **75% del lienzo**: pecho, abrigo y fondo son la misma región, porque el cuello, el hombro y el borde del abrigo tienen huecos. Solo el pelo queda cerrado. Así que retiro lo que te propuse: colorear esto por regiones no funciona, y no quiero venderte algo que sé que va a fallar.

Con lo que hay, tres caminos reales:

**1. Cierras tú la línea.** En cualquier programa de dibujo, una capa de "line art" con los contornos cerrados y sin el rayado del sombreado. Con eso el relleno automático sí funciona, y a partir de ahí el color es mío para siempre: misma paleta, cero deriva, las cincuenta piezas iguales. Es la opción que mejor queda y la que más trabajo te cuesta.

**2. Pintas los planos tú, yo compongo.** No hace falta que cierres nada: una capa aparte con manchas planas de color (piel, pelo, abrigo, pantalón) y yo la monto debajo de tu línea. Menos trabajo que la 1 y el resultado sigue siendo tuyo.

**3. Seguimos con la versión que ya coloreó la IA** —el del abrigo— como referencia, y aceptamos que Seedream endurece un poco el trazo. Es la más rápida y la única que no te pide dibujar más.

Mi recomendación es la **2**: te cuesta poco, la línea sigue siendo tuya al 100% y a mí me deja el control del color.

Dos cosas sueltas: mándame el line art como **archivo**, no captura del PDF — de la captura pierdo la mitad de la resolución. Y he visto de refilón que tu GDD tiene tipografías y una paleta definidas (Roboto/Inter, ese `#FF3A3A`); si me pasas ese documento, la app la monto con **tus** colores y tus fuentes en vez de con los que me inventé yo.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-08</sub>

Funciona.

Cómo lo he resuelto, porque el relleno simple sí fallaba: primero engordo tu trazo hasta que la silueta cierra, separo figura de papel, y luego los dos rellenos van por separado — el fondo ya no puede colarse por el cuello ni la piel escaparse al papel. Dentro de la figura siembro puntos ("esto es piel", "esto es abrigo") y el color crece desde ahí frenándose en tu línea. Donde hay hueco, la frontera cae a medio camino en vez de derramarse.

Lo que esto te da:

- **Tu dibujo intacto.** No hay ni un píxel redibujado. Cero deriva de estilo, para siempre.
- **La paleta es una línea de código.** Cambiar el tono de piel o el color del abrigo de los cuatro personajes son ocho números. Y saco la misma imagen con fondo transparente, o verde, o del tamaño que necesite la app.
- **Lo repito gratis.** Una vez colocadas las siembras de un personaje, colorear otra pose suya es un segundo.

Y lo que **no** te da, que es importante: esto colorea lo que tú dibujas. No inventa poses. Para las doce piezas de cada personaje harían falta doce dibujos tuyos, y eso es mucho lápiz.

Así que la jugada buena creo que es combinar las dos cosas: **usar esto como referencia para OpenArt** en vez de la versión que coloreó la IA. Le estarías dando tu línea y tus colores exactos, no una reinterpretación con contorno negro y contraste duro. Es bastante probable que las piezas generadas salgan mucho más cerca.

Dime si tiro por ahí. Y dos cosillas: el color está plano, sin sombras — se pueden añadir después, pero primero quería los planos limpios. Y si tienes los line art **sin el nombre escrito al lado**, mejor; ese texto forma parte del dibujo y me tocaría borrarlo a mano.

## Claude  <sub>2026-09-09</sub>

El proceso funciona de punta a punta. Con estos cuatro he probado las tres cosas:

**Croma.** Mido el verde real del fondo, abro una rampa suave de alfa y le quito el reflejo verde al filo del pelo. Por eso no hay halo y los mechones sueltos sobreviven. Nada que ver con los recortes sobre blanco.

**Alineado.** Mido la coronilla y el ancho de cabeza de cada pieza y las reescalo a un lienzo común. Aquí sí hay un límite: en Reiko el ancho lo marca la melena, no la cara, así que si en una pieza ladea la cabeza el ajuste baila un poco. Se nota poco, pero si al montar el chat da saltos, lo afino.

**Avatares.** Salen del busto, recortados y centrados en la cara. Cero créditos.

Dos cosas antes de seguir. La cuarta imagen que me pasaste, la de los cuadritos grises, **vuelve a traer la transparencia pintada** — no es alfa. Ignórala y usa la versión verde de esa misma pieza; si no la tienes, la relanzo.

Y **pásame el resto de la tanda**. Son 24 gestos y solo me han llegado cuatro. Con todos, proceso los 24 de una tacada y monto la pantalla de chat: fondo tuyo, burbujas, avatar, y la cara del personaje cambiando según lo que diga. Eso ya es el juego.

src/engine/   chat.js  story.js  state.js  portraits.js  art.js  conditions.js  audio.js
src/ui/       chat-view.js  hub.js  apps.js  shell.js  onboarding.js  album-util.js
data/story/   kenta.js  lara.js  reiko.js  ryu.js  common.js  filler.js  secret.js
```

Así que cambio la propuesta: en vez de montarte otra pantalla de chat suelta, **meto el arte nuevo en el juego que ya tienes**. Déjame leer `portraits.js` y `chat-view.js` para ver cómo está pidiendo las imágenes ahora mismo, y te digo qué encaja directo y qué hay que tocar.

El croma lo resolví cambiando el criterio: en vez de medir la distancia al verde exacto del fondo, mido **cuánto tira a verde** cada píxel — así cae igual el croma brillante y el rim verde oscuro del contorno, y tu paleta (crema, piel, negro, rosa) no se toca. ## Claude  <sub>2026-09-09</sub>

Lanzados los dos de Reiko — `relief` y `scared` — para validar el tono antes de tirar los seis restantes. 40 créditos.

Los prompts los escribí desde lo que dice `calls.js`, no desde una idea genérica de "retrato triste":

- **relief**: cámara frontal mala, de noche, en una calle vacía. Ojeras, pelo sin arreglar, piel húmeda. Llorando y sonriendo a la vez, mirando al objetivo. Solo la luz de una farola y el brillo de la pantalla.
- **scared**: pasillo institucional a oscuras. Pálida, mandíbula tensa, mirando *más allá* de la cámara a algo que hay detrás. Luz dura desde arriba, grano y artefactos de compresión, como si la señal se estuviera yendo.

En los dos mantengo tu estilo y tu trazo, pero bajado de tono. Sin croma esta vez: aquí el fondo oscuro forma parte de la imagen, porque `realFace` llena todo el hueco del vídeo.

De paso he ampliado `portraits.js`: `art.real` ahora acepta `{ relief, scared }` además de una ruta suelta, así que el final bueno y el malo ya no comparten cara.

Cuando los veas, dime si el tono es el que buscas. Si sí, van los seis de Kenta, Lara y Ryu — 120 créditos más.
